5.4
Sharpe ratio upper bound and asset pricing theory

Our theory is also useful in informing us the clairvoyant Sharpe ratio of the underlying assets (i.e., the true Sharpe ratio one would achieve if the true distribution of these assets is known). We estimate the clairvoyant Sharpe ratio as a function of the number of assets
N
based on the empirical performance in Figure
16
.

To proceed, we first make an assumption about the functional form of
\theta
. We assume that the clairvoyant Sharpe ratio of
N
assets is

\sqrt{\theta(N)}=\sqrt{\theta_{1}}e^{-\lambda(N-1)}+\sqrt{\bar{\theta}}(1-e^{-\lambda(N-1)})

(18)

where
\sqrt{\theta_{1}}
is the Sharpe ratio of a single asset,
\sqrt{\bar{\theta}}
is the maximum clairvoyant Sharpe ratio in the economy, and
\lambda
is the speed parameter. We estimate these three parameters by fitting the empirical performance curve in Figure
16
based on Equation (
10
). To accomplish this task, we also need to estimate the factor strength of our test assets, which is accomplished in Section
5.1
.

Figure
17
illustrates the calibrated clairvoyant Sharpe ratio (the blue line), the implied model performance from Theorem
3
(the green line), and the actual model performance. As we can see, it completely meets our expectation that it first quickly increases with
N
and then holds constant. The three parameters values are:
\sqrt{\theta_{1}}=0.4526
,
\sqrt{\bar{\theta}}=1.4431
, and
\lambda=0.0285
.

Figure 17
:
Blue: clairvoyant Sharpe ratio
\sqrt{\theta(N)}=0.4526e^{-0.0285(N-1)}+1.4431(1-e^{-0.0285(N-1)})
. Green: Asymptotic limits of out-of-sample Sharpe ratio. Red dots: out-of-sample Sharpe ratio in the data.

This calibration exercise shows that the upper limit of the clairvoyant Sharpe ratio among our test assets is 1.44, which is informative about the asset pricing model needed to explain these test assets. An important question in the asset pricing literature is how many factors should be in an asset pricing mode. The CAPM has a single factor. The Fama-French three and five factor models have 3 and 5 factors, respectively. Given the Sharpe ratio upper bound of 1.44, we can back out the number of independent factors needed to generate this high Sharpe ratio. Specifically, the market factor has a Sharpe ratio of 0.42 during our sample period. The Sharpe ratio upper bound is 3.5 times that of the market factors. If we assume the asset pricing factors have the same Sharpe ratio as the market, then we need 12 independent asset pricing factors to reach the theoretical Sharpe ratio upper bound. Interestingly, our estimate, based on an entirely different approach, is similar to some other estimates in the asset pricing literature. For example,
Freyberger et al., (2020)
find 13 firm characteristics have incremental explanatory power of the cross-section of expected returns.
DeMiguel et al., (2020)
find that in the presence of transaction costs, 15 firm characteristics are jointly significant.

The speed parameter
\lambda
informs us how fast the clairvoyant Sharpe ratio increases with the number of assets. To reach half of the Sharp ratio upper bound, the portfolio needs to have around 13 assets. At the optimal low-dimensional point, when
N=39
, the clairvoyant Sharpe ratio is 1.11, which is about three quarters of the upper limit. However, Figure
17
shows that we cannot achieve such a theoretical Sharpe ratio empirically due to estimation errors in the low-dimensional regime. When
N=121
, entering the high-dimensional regime, the clairvoyant Sharpe ratio is 1.42, reaching 98% of its upper limit. This indicates that the improvement in the empirical out-of-sample Sharpe ratio in the high-dimensional regime is largely due to the reduction in estimation error instead of the increase in the clairvoyant Sharpe ratio.

6
Conclusion and Future Work

In this study, we explored several factors affecting the out-of-sample performance of mean-variance portfolios. By quantifying these key parameters, we especially unveiled the pivotal role of clairvoyant Sharpe ratio and factor strength in portfolio optimization. Our findings highlight that stronger factors can lead to better performance in an overparameterization regime compared to an underparameterization regime. This counterintuitive result suggests that embracing higher complexity, under certain conditions, can be beneficial. Our work provides a framework for practitioners to carefully consider the trade-off between the amount of available data and the flexibility of incorporating information in portfolio construction. Looking ahead, there are several promising avenues for further research. One direction is to extend our quantitative analysis to other types of portfolios, such as the minimum variance portfolio. Investigating whether similar patterns of overparameterization and factor strength influence their performance could yield valuable insights. Another exciting prospect is to delve into the “double descent” in the context of applying artificial intelligence to large-scale portfolio theory. Understanding how AI-driven models can harness complexity without succumbing to overfitting could revolutionize portfolio management practices. Expanding our study in these directions may contribute significantly to both theoretical advancements and practical applications in financial portfolio optimization.

Supplementary Material to ““Double Descent” in Portfolio Optimization: Dance between Theoretical Sharpe Ratio and Estimation Accuracy”

Yonghe Lu, Yanrong Yang, Terry Zhang

College of Business and Economics at
The Australian National University

August 24, 2026

This document provides supplementary materials, including additional explanations, additional empirical results, proofs, theoretical findings for the ridge-regularized estimator, and references.

Appendix A
Closer Look at Mean and SD of OOS Portfolio Return

We delve into the theoretical results to explore the underlying reasons behind the surprising ascent in the Sharpe ratio. We separately present the asymptotic results for the mean and standard deviation of out-of-sample return.

Proposition 3
.

Under Assumptions
B1
-
B3
, it holds in probability that

\mathbb{E}\left[\widehat{{\bm{\omega}}^{\ast}_{{}^{+}}}^{\top}\bm{r}_{0}\Big|{\bm{R}}\right]\rightarrow\mu\left({\tilde{\theta}},\tilde{\phi},\rho,\sigma^{2}\right)=\left\{\begin{aligned} &\frac{\sigma}{\sqrt{{\tilde{\theta}}}}\frac{{\tilde{\theta}}}{1-\rho},&\text{for }\rho<1,\\
&\frac{\sigma}{\sqrt{{\tilde{\theta}}}}\frac{{\tilde{\theta}}\rho(\tilde{\phi}+1)^{2}}{\left(\rho-1\right)(\tilde{\phi}+\rho)^{2}},&\text{for }\rho>1;\end{aligned}\right.

(A.1)

\mathbb{SD}\left[\widehat{{\bm{\omega}}^{\ast}_{{}^{+}}}^{\top}\bm{r}_{0}\Big|{\bm{R}}\right]\rightarrow\sigma\left({\tilde{\theta}},\tilde{\phi},\rho,\sigma^{2}\right)=\left\{\begin{aligned} &\frac{\sigma}{\sqrt{{\tilde{\theta}}}}\sqrt{\frac{{\tilde{\theta}}+\rho}{\left(1-\rho\right)^{3}}},\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\text{for }\rho<1,\\
&\frac{\sigma}{\sqrt{{\tilde{\theta}}}}\sqrt{\frac{\rho{\tilde{\theta}}\tilde{\phi}^{2}(\tilde{\phi}+1)^{2}}{(\rho-1)(\tilde{\phi}+\rho)^{4}}+\frac{\rho^{2}{\tilde{\theta}}(\tilde{\phi}+1)^{2}}{(\rho-1)^{3}(\tilde{\phi}+\rho)^{2}}+\frac{\rho}{(\rho-1)^{3}}},\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\text{for }\rho>1.\\
\end{aligned}\right.

(A.2)

The following figure shows how the mean and standard deviation change with different values of
\rho
when
{\tilde{\theta}}
is 16 for various factor strengths. For
\rho<1
, both the mean and standard deviation increase with
\rho
and are consistently overestimated (the true values of the mean and standard deviation are
\sigma\sqrt{{\tilde{\theta}}}
and
\sigma
, respectively). When
\rho>1
, both the mean and standard deviation decrease. Initially, they are overestimated but then become underestimated as
\rho
increases. Overall, the Sharpe ratio rises in the
\rho>1
region because the mean decreases more slowly than the standard deviation. However, as the mean decreases more significantly, the Sharpe ratio eventually falls again after its initial rise.

Figure A.1
:
Asymptotic mean and standard deviation of OOS return in Proposition
3
.

Appendix B
Bias-Variance Decomposition

For an estimator
\widehat{{\bm{\omega}}^{\ast}}
, which is a function of the observed data
{\bm{R}}
, the out-of-sample prediction loss can be decomposed as:

\displaystyle L_{\bm{R}}\left(\widehat{{\bm{\omega}}^{\ast}};{\bm{\omega}^{\ast}}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\widehat{{\bm{\omega}}^{\ast}}^{\top}\bm{r}_{0}-{{\bm{\omega}^{\ast}}}^{\top}\bm{r}_{0}\right)^{2}\Big|{\bm{R}}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\left(\widehat{{\bm{\omega}}^{\ast}}-{\bm{\omega}^{\ast}}\right)^{\top}\bm{r}_{0}\right)^{2}\Bigg|{\bm{R}}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\widehat{{\bm{\omega}}^{\ast}}-{{\bm{\omega}^{\ast}}}\right)^{\top}\bm{r}_{0}\bm{r}_{0}^{\top}\left(\widehat{{\bm{\omega}}^{\ast}}-{{\bm{\omega}^{\ast}}}\right)\Bigg|{\bm{R}}\right]

\displaystyle=\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\widehat{{\bm{\omega}}^{\ast}}\big|{\bm{R}}\right]-{{\bm{\omega}^{\ast}}}\right)^{\top}\left({\bm{\Sigma}}+{\bm{\mu}}{\bm{\mu}}^{\top}\right)\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\widehat{{\bm{\omega}}^{\ast}}\big|{\bm{R}}\right]-{{\bm{\omega}^{\ast}}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\text{ }+\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\widehat{{\bm{\omega}}^{\ast}}-\mathbb{E}\left[\widehat{{\bm{\omega}}^{\ast}}\big|{\bm{R}}\right]\right)^{\top}\left({\bm{\Sigma}}+{\bm{\mu}}{\bm{\mu}}^{\top}\right)\left(\widehat{{\bm{\omega}}^{\ast}}-\mathbb{E}\left[\widehat{{\bm{\omega}}^{\ast}}\big|{\bm{R}}\right]\right)\Big|{\bm{R}}\right]

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\widehat{{\bm{\omega}}^{\ast}}\big|{\bm{R}}\right]-{{\bm{\omega}^{\ast}}}\right)^{\top}\left({\bm{\Sigma}}+{\bm{\mu}}{\bm{\mu}}^{\top}\right)\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\widehat{{\bm{\omega}}^{\ast}}\big|{\bm{R}}\right]-{{\bm{\omega}^{\ast}}}\right)}_{B_{{\bm{R}}}\left[\widehat{{\bm{\omega}}^{\ast}};{{\bm{\omega}^{\ast}}}\right]}+\underbrace{\mathrm{Tr}\left[\mathrm{Cov}\left[\widehat{\bm{\omega^{\ast}}}\big|{\bm{R}}\right]\left({\bm{\Sigma}}+{\bm{\mu}}{\bm{\mu}}^{\top}\right)\right]}_{V_{{\bm{R}}}\left[\widehat{{\bm{\omega}}^{\ast}};{{\bm{\omega}^{\ast}}}\right]}

It is evident in the second-to-last line that
\mathbb{E}\left[\widehat{{\bm{\omega}}^{\ast}}\big|{\bm{R}}\right]=\widehat{{\bm{\omega}}^{\ast}}
, as
\widehat{{\bm{\omega}}^{\ast}}
is entirely determined by
{\bm{R}}
. Consequently, given
{\bm{R}}
, the expectation of
\widehat{{\bm{\omega}}^{\ast}}
is the estimator itself. This leads to
\widehat{{\bm{\omega}}^{\ast}}-\mathbb{E}\left[\widehat{{\bm{\omega}}^{\ast}}\big|{\bm{R}}\right]=0
, thereby confirming that
{V_{{\bm{R}}}\left[\widehat{{\bm{\omega}}^{\ast}};{{\bm{\omega}^{\ast}}}\right]}=0
. To sum up, the out-of-sample prediction loss for estimator
\widehat{{\bm{\omega}}^{\ast}}
can also be expressed as:

\displaystyle L_{{\bm{R}}}\left[\widehat{{\bm{\omega}}^{\ast}};{{\bm{\omega}^{\ast}}}\right]={B_{{\bm{R}}}\left[\widehat{{\bm{\omega}}^{\ast}};{{\bm{\omega}^{\ast}}}\right]}=\left(\widehat{{\bm{\omega}}^{\ast}}-{{\bm{\omega}^{\ast}}}\right)^{\top}\left({\bm{\Sigma}}+{\bm{\mu}}{\bm{\mu}}^{\top}\right)\left(\widehat{{\bm{\omega}}^{\ast}}-{{\bm{\omega}^{\ast}}}\right).

Appendix C
Proofs

C.1
Lemma 1

Lemma 1

(Quadratic-form-close-to-the-trace, trace lemma,
Bai and Silverstein, (2010)
Lemma B.26)
.

Let
{\bm{x}}

\in

\mathbb{R}^{p}
have independent entries
x_{i}
of zero mean, unit variance and
\mathbb{E}\left[|x_{i}|^{K}\right]\leq c_{K}
for some
K\geq 1
. Then for
{\bm{A}}\in\mathbb{R}^{p\times p}
and
k\geq 1
,

\mathbb{E}\left[\left|{\bm{x}}^{\top}{\bm{A}}{\bm{x}}-\Tr({\bm{A}})\right|^{k}\right]\leq C_{k}\left[\left(c_{4}\Tr({\bm{A}}{\bm{A}}^{\top})\right)^{k/2}+c_{2k}\Tr({\bm{A}}{\bm{A}}^{\top})^{k/2}\right],

for some constant
C_{k}>0
independent of
p
. For generic
{\bm{A}}
, when the entries of
{\bm{x}}
have finite fourth moment,
\Var[{\bm{x}}^{\top}{\bm{A}}{\bm{x}}/p]=O(p^{-1})
.
Consequently,

\dfrac{1}{p}{\bm{x}}^{\top}{\bm{A}}{\bm{x}}-\dfrac{1}{p}\Tr({\bm{A}})=O_{p}\left(p^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right).

(C.3)

Remark 1

(For Lemma
1
)
.

Assume we have another vector
{\bm{y}}
that is independent of
{\bm{x}}
, but has the same properties with
{\bm{x}}
, then,
\dfrac{1}{p}{\bm{x}}^{\top}{\bm{A}}{\bm{y}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle i.p.}}{{\longrightarrow}}0
.

C.2
Proofs for Proposition
1
, Theorem
1
, Theorem
2

Proof:
.

Let
{\widehat{\bm{m}}}={\widehat{{\bm{\mu}}}}-{\bm{\mu}}
represent the centered sample mean vector. In accordance with the Data Generating Process (DGP) Assumption
A2
, we know that
\sqrt{T}{\widehat{\bm{m}}}
has a zero mean and
{\bm{\Sigma}}
(here
\bm{I}_{N}
) as its covariance matrix.

To prove for Proposition
1
, we can work out the asymptotic limit of
{\widehat{\theta}}_{s}
first, then find a consistent estimator for
{\tilde{\theta}}
reversely. We know that
{\widehat{\theta}}_{s}={\widehat{{\bm{\mu}}}}^{\top}{\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}}^{+}{\widehat{{\bm{\mu}}}}
can be decomposed into:

\displaystyle{\widehat{\theta}}_{s}

\displaystyle=\left({\bm{\mu}}+{\widehat{\bm{m}}}\right)^{\top}{\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}}^{+}\left({\bm{\mu}}+{\widehat{\bm{m}}}\right)

\displaystyle={\bm{\mu}}^{\top}{\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}}^{+}{\bm{\mu}}+{\widehat{\bm{m}}}^{\top}{\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}}^{+}{\widehat{\bm{m}}}+2{\bm{\mu}}^{\top}{\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}}^{+}{\widehat{\bm{m}}}.

For Theorem
2
, recall that the pseudoinverse estimator is defined by

\displaystyle\widehat{{\bm{\omega}}^{\ast}_{{}^{+}}}=\dfrac{\sigma}{\sqrt{{\widehat{\theta}}}}{\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}}^{+}{\widehat{{\bm{\mu}}}},

then the prediction loss of the pseudoinverse estimator can be expanded using key intermediate terms

\displaystyle L_{{\bm{R}}}\left[\widehat{{\bm{\omega}}^{\ast}_{{}^{+}}};{{\bm{\omega}^{\ast}}}\right]

\displaystyle={B_{{\bm{R}}}\left(\widehat{{\bm{\omega}}^{\ast}_{{}^{+}}};{{\bm{\omega}^{\ast}}}\right)}

\displaystyle=\left(\widehat{{\bm{\omega}}^{\ast}_{{}^{+}}}-{{\bm{\omega}^{\ast}}}\right)^{\top}\left({\bm{\Sigma}}+{\bm{\mu}}{\bm{\mu}}^{\top}\right)\left(\widehat{{\bm{\omega}}^{\ast}_{{}^{+}}}-{{\bm{\omega}^{\ast}}}\right)

\displaystyle=\sigma^{2}\left[\dfrac{{\widehat{{\bm{\mu}}}}^{\top}{\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}}^{+}\bm{I}_{N}{\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}}^{+}{\widehat{{\bm{\mu}}}}}{{\widehat{\theta}}}-2\dfrac{{\widehat{{\bm{\mu}}}}^{\top}{\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}}^{+}{{\bm{\mu}}}}{\sqrt{{\widehat{\theta}}}\sqrt{\theta}}+1\right.

\displaystyle\quad\text{ }+\left.\dfrac{\left({\widehat{{\bm{\mu}}}}^{\top}{\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}}^{+}{\bm{\mu}}\right)^{2}}{{\widehat{\theta}}}-2\dfrac{{\widehat{{\bm{\mu}}}}^{\top}{\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}}^{+}{\bm{\mu}}\sqrt{\theta}}{\sqrt{{\widehat{\theta}}}}+{\theta}\right].

For Theorem
1
, the Sharpe ratio of the pseudoinverse estimator can be expanded as

\displaystyle SR\left[\widehat{{\bm{\omega}}^{\ast}_{{}^{+}}}\right]=\dfrac{{\widehat{{\bm{\mu}}}}^{\top}{\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}}^{+}{\bm{\mu}}}{\sqrt{{\widehat{{\bm{\mu}}}}^{\top}{\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}}^{+}\bm{I}_{N}{\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}}^{+}{\widehat{{\bm{\mu}}}}}}.

Determining the asymptotic limit of the above three statistics simplifies to the task of determining the limits of six specific terms, which we denote as
D1
to
D6
:
D1={\bm{\mu}}^{\top}{\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}}^{+}{\bm{\mu}}
,
D2={\widehat{\bm{m}}}^{\top}{\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}}^{+}{\widehat{\bm{m}}}
,
D3={\bm{\mu}}^{\top}{\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}}^{+}{\widehat{\bm{m}}}
,
D4={\bm{\mu}}^{\top}{\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}}^{+}\bm{I}_{N}{\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}}^{+}{\bm{\mu}}
,
D5={\widehat{\bm{m}}}^{\top}{\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}}^{+}\bm{I}_{N}{\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}}^{+}{\widehat{\bm{m}}}
,

D6={\bm{\mu}}^{\top}{\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}}^{+}\bm{I}_{N}{\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}}^{+}{\widehat{\bm{m}}}
. In the following analysis, we will derive the limits of these six terms respectively.

The proof becomes more straightforward when formulated in matrix terms. Consider the
T\times N
data matrix
{\bm{R}}
, with its
t^{\text{th}}
row represents by
\bm{r}_{t}
. Similarly,
\bm{Y}
denotes the
T\times N
data matrix, where the
t^{\text{th}}
row is
{\bm{y}}_{t}
. Then,
{\bm{R}}=\bm{e}{\bm{\mu}}^{\top}+\bm{Y}\bm{I}_{N}
, where
\bm{e}
is a vector of ones, with each entry equal to 1. Additionally, we define
\bm{H}=\bm{I}-\frac{\bm{e}\bm{e}^{\top}}{T}
, then

\displaystyle{\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}}

\displaystyle=\dfrac{1}{T-1}\left({\bm{R}}-\bar{{\bm{R}}}\right)^{\top}\left({\bm{R}}-\bar{{\bm{R}}}\right)

\displaystyle=\dfrac{1}{T-1}\left(\bm{H}{\bm{R}}\right)^{\top}\left(\bm{H}{\bm{R}}\right)

\displaystyle=\dfrac{1}{T-1}\left(\bm{H}\bm{Y}\bm{I}_{N}\right)^{\top}\left(\bm{H}\bm{Y}\bm{I}_{N}\right)

\displaystyle=\dfrac{1}{T-1}\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{H}\bm{Y}.

Note that the matrix
\bm{H}
is formulated as the identity matrix minus a rank one matrix. When considering the limit as
T\rightarrow\infty
, the difference between
1/T
and
1/(T-1)
becomes inconsequential. Hence we define
{\bm{\mathcal{S}}}=T^{-1}\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{H}\bm{Y}
and consider
{\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}}={\bm{\mathcal{S}}}
. Consequently, the pseudoinverse of
{\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}}
is given by
{\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}}^{+}={\bm{\mathcal{S}}}^{+}
.

Limit of
D1
: Under Assumptions
A1
-
A2
, we consider the ratio of
D1
to
{\bm{\mu}}^{\top}{\bm{\Sigma}}^{-1}{\bm{\mu}}
. We have

\frac{{\bm{\mu}}^{\top}{\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}}^{+}{\bm{\mu}}}{{\bm{\mu}}^{\top}{\bm{\mu}}}={\bm{\nu}}^{\top}\left(\frac{1}{T}\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{H}\bm{Y}\right)^{+}{\bm{\nu}}={\bm{\nu}}^{\top}{\bm{\mathcal{S}}}^{+}{\bm{\nu}},

where
{\bm{\nu}}={\bm{\mu}}/\left\|{\bm{\mu}}\right\|_{2}
is a vector of
\ell_{2}
norm 1.

If
{\bm{O}}
is an orthogonal matrix, then
{\bm{O}}^{\top}\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{H}\bm{Y}{\bm{O}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\cal{L}}}}{{=}}\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{H}\bm{Y}
. Since
\bm{Y}
is full of
i.i.d.
random variables with
\mathbb{E}[y_{11}]=0
,
\mathbb{E}[y_{11}^{2}]=1
and
\mathbb{E}[y_{11}^{4}]<\infty
, it is invariant (in law) by left and right rotation. Also, the eigenvalues and eigenvectors of
\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{H}\bm{Y}
are
independent and its matrix of eigenvectors (i.e., eigenmatrix) is uniformly (i.e., Haar) distributed on the orthogonal group. To see this, we can refer to
Bai et al., (2007)
, where the authors proved that the eigenmatrix of large sample covariance matrix (
N
increases with sample size proportionally) is nearly Haar distributed when the population covariance matrix is a multiple of the identity matrix. Let us write a spectral decomposition of
\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{H}\bm{Y}

{{\bm{\mathcal{S}}}}=\frac{1}{T}\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{H}\bm{Y}=\sum_{i=1}^{N}s_{i}{\bm{u}}_{i}{\bm{u}}_{i}^{\top}.

We know that
s_{i}\neq 0
for all
i
when
\rho<1
, so

{\bm{\nu}}^{\top}{{\bm{\mathcal{S}}}}^{+}{\bm{\nu}}={\bm{\nu}}^{\top}{{\bm{\mathcal{S}}}}^{-1}{\bm{\nu}}=\sum_{i=1}^{N}\frac{1}{s_{i}}\left({\bm{\nu}}^{\top}{\bm{u}}_{i}\right)^{2}.

Based on Lemma
1
, we claim that

\dfrac{1}{N}\left({\sqrt{N}}{\bm{\nu}}\right)^{\top}{{\bm{\mathcal{S}}}}^{-1}\left({\sqrt{N}}{\bm{\nu}}\right)-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\frac{1}{s_{i}}\rightarrow 0,\quad\text{in probability}.

To see this, note that
\mathbb{E}(({\bm{\nu}}^{\top}{\bm{u}}_{i})^{2})=\|{\bm{\nu}}\|_{2}^{2}/N=1/N
because
{\bm{u}}_{i}
is uniformly distributed on the unit
sphere when
\bm{\Upsilon}
(the matrix containing the
{\bm{u}}_{i}
) is Haar distributed on the orthogonal group. Also, when
\rho<1
,

\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\frac{1}{s_{i}}=\int\frac{1}{s}dF_{{\bm{\mathcal{S}}}}(s),

where
F_{{\bm{\mathcal{S}}}}
is the spectral measure of
T^{-1}\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{H}\bm{Y}
. Recall the Marčenko-Pastur equation, the Stieltjes transform of the the spectral distribution of
T^{-1}\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{H}\bm{Y}
tend to
m_{F_{MP,\rho}}(z)
in probability and
m_{F_{MP,\rho}}(z)
satisfies

m_{F_{MP,\rho}}(z)=\dfrac{1}{1-\rho-\rho zm_{F_{MP,\rho}}(z)-z}.

Thus, when
\rho<1

\frac{{\bm{\mu}}^{\top}{\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}}^{+}{\bm{\mu}}}{{\bm{\mu}}^{\top}\bm{I}_{N}^{-1}{\bm{\mu}}}\rightarrow m_{F_{MP,\rho}}(0)=\dfrac{1}{1-\rho}.

When
\rho>1
,
s_{i}\neq 0
for all
i\leq T
and we now claim that

\dfrac{1}{N}\left({\sqrt{N}}{\bm{\nu}}\right)^{\top}{{\bm{\mathcal{S}}}}^{+}\left({\sqrt{N}}{\bm{\nu}}\right)-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{T}\frac{1}{s_{i}}\rightarrow 0,\quad\text{in probability}.

Let
t_{i}
,
i=1,...,T
denote the eigenvalues of
\bm{H}\bm{Y}\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{H}/N
. Then we may write
s_{i}=(N/T)t_{i}
,
i=1,...T
, and

\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{T}\frac{1}{s_{i}}=\frac{1}{N}\frac{T}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{T}\frac{1}{t_{i}}=\left(\frac{T}{N}\right)^{2}\int\frac{1}{t}dF_{\bm{H}\bm{Y}\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{H}/N}(t),

where
F_{\bm{H}\bm{Y}\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{H}/N}
is the spectral measure of
\bm{H}\bm{Y}\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{H}/N
. Now as
T/N\rightarrow 1/\rho<1
, by the same arguments as above, we may conclude that in probability

\frac{{\bm{\mu}}^{\top}{\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}}^{+}{\bm{\mu}}}{{\bm{\mu}}^{\top}\bm{I}_{N}^{-1}{\bm{\mu}}}\rightarrow\left(\frac{1}{\rho}\right)^{2}\dfrac{1}{1-1/\rho}=\dfrac{1}{\rho(\rho-1)}.

To sum up,

\frac{{\bm{\mu}}^{\top}{\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}}^{+}{\bm{\mu}}}{{\bm{\mu}}^{\top}\bm{I}_{N}^{-1}{\bm{\mu}}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle i.p.}}{{\longrightarrow}}\left\{\begin{aligned} &\dfrac{1}{1-\rho},&\text{for }\rho<1,\\
&\dfrac{1}{\rho(\rho-1)},&\text{for }\rho>1.\end{aligned}\right.

Under Assumption
A3
, where
{\bm{\mu}}^{\top}\bm{I}_{N}^{-1}{\bm{\mu}}=\theta\rightarrow{\tilde{\theta}}
, we get

{\bm{\mu}}^{\top}{\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}}^{+}{\bm{\mu}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle i.p.}}{{\longrightarrow}}\left\{\begin{aligned} &\dfrac{1}{1-\rho}{\tilde{\theta}},&\text{for }\rho<1,\\
&\dfrac{1}{\rho(\rho-1)}{\tilde{\theta}},&\text{for }\rho>1.\end{aligned}\right.

Limit of
D2
: Recall that
{\widehat{\bm{m}}}={\bm{\Sigma}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{e}/T
represents the vector of column means of
{\bm{R}}
, adjusted by subtracting the mean vector
{\bm{\mu}}
. We have

\displaystyle{\widehat{\bm{m}}}^{\top}{\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}}^{+}{\widehat{\bm{m}}}=

\displaystyle\left(\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{e}/T\right)^{\top}\left(\frac{1}{T}\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{H}\bm{Y}\right)^{+}\left(\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{e}/T\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{e}/T\right)^{\top}\left(\frac{1}{T}\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{H}\bm{Y}\right)^{+}\left(\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{e}/T\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\dfrac{1}{T}\left(\sqrt{T}{\bar{{\bm{s}}}}\right)^{\top}{\bm{\mathcal{S}}}^{+}\left(\sqrt{T}{\bar{{\bm{s}}}}\right),

where
{\bar{{\bm{s}}}}=\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{e}/T
. This framework parallels the quadratic form of
{\bm{\mathcal{S}}}
in the proof for
D1
, where a fixed unit vector
{\bm{\nu}}
is present on both the left and right sides of the equation, thereby creating a quadratic form. However, in this case,
\sqrt{T}{\bar{{\bm{s}}}}
is a vector of
N
random variables, with each entry following normal distribution
\mathcal{N}(0,1)
. In this case, we can consider the trace Lemma
1
in Lemma B.26 of
Bai and Silverstein, (2010)
, which is fundamental in establishing initial heuristics for key identities of random matrix theory. It utilizes the approximation
\frac{1}{p}{\bm{x}}^{\top}{\bm{A}}{\bm{x}}\simeq\frac{1}{p}\Tr({\bm{A}})
for
{\bm{x}}
with independent zero-mean unit-variance entries and independent of
{\bm{A}}
. Returning to our case, it is clear that
{\bar{{\bm{s}}}}
is not independent of
{\bm{\mathcal{S}}}
(unless we assume
{\bm{y}}_{t}
is multivariate Gaussian), thereby we can not apply this lemma directly. However, it is noteworthy that
T\times{\bar{{\bm{s}}}}^{\top}{\bm{\mathcal{S}}}^{-1}{\bar{{\bm{s}}}}
is the Hotelling’s
T^{2}
statistic. This aspect has been extensively investigated under large dimension scenarios by
Pan and Zhou, (2011)
. By Theorem 2 in their work, under the same moment assumptions in Assumption
A2
, they suggested that
{\bar{{\bm{s}}}}/\left\|{\bar{{\bm{s}}}}\right\|
can be viewed as a fixed unit vector when dealing with
{\bar{{\bm{s}}}}^{\top}{\bm{\mathcal{S}}}^{-1}{\bar{{\bm{s}}}}/\left\|{\bar{{\bm{s}}}}\right\|^{2}
even if
{\bar{{\bm{s}}}}
is not independent of
{\bm{\mathcal{S}}}
. This enables us to find the limiting behaviour of
{\bar{{\bm{s}}}}^{\top}{\bm{\mathcal{S}}}^{-1}{\bar{{\bm{s}}}}
by using the same proof argument as in the proof for
D1
.

More broadly,
Pan and Zhou, (2011)
have elucidated the asymptotic distributions of random quadratic forms, particularly those involving the sample mean and sample covariance. For a thorough understanding of their findings, readers are directed to Theorem 2 in their paper. The result related to our proof for D2, D3, D5, D6 (D5, D6 are defined in the proof of Theorem
2
) is as follows: given that
f(x)
is analytic within an open interval, the expression

\sqrt{T}\left[\dfrac{{\bar{{\bm{s}}}}^{\top}f({\bm{\mathcal{S}}}){\bar{{\bm{s}}}}}{\left\|{\bar{{\bm{s}}}}\right\|^{2}}-\int f(s)dF_{MP,\rho_{T}}(s)\right]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\cal{D}}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathcal{N}\left(0,\mathbb{V}\right)

(C.4)

holds, where
\mathbb{V}=\dfrac{2}{\rho}\left[\int f^{2}(s)dF_{MP,\rho}(s)-\left(\int f(s)dF_{MP,\rho}(s)\right)^{2}\right]
.
Furthermore,

\sqrt{T}\left[\left\|{\bar{{\bm{s}}}}\right\|^{2}-\rho_{T}\right]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\cal{D}}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathcal{N}(0,2\rho).

(C.5)

This implies that
\left\|{\bar{{\bm{s}}}}\right\|^{2}
converges towards
\rho
in probability, irrespective of whether
\rho
is larger than 1 or not. And now it becomes clear that the only difference between the limiting behaviors of
{\widehat{\bm{m}}}^{\top}{\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}}^{+}{\widehat{\bm{m}}}
and
{\bm{\mu}}^{\top}{\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}}^{+}{\bm{\mu}}/{\bm{\mu}}^{\top}\bm{I}_{N}^{-1}{\bm{\mu}}
lies in the multiplication factor of
\left\|{\bar{{\bm{s}}}}\right\|^{2}
. Thus it is easy to derive from results of
D1
that
{\widehat{\bm{m}}}^{\top}{\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}}^{+}{\widehat{\bm{m}}}\rightarrow{\rho}/{(1-\rho)}
when
\rho<1
, and
{\widehat{\bm{m}}}^{\top}{\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}}^{+}{\widehat{\bm{m}}}\rightarrow{1}/{(\rho-1)}
when
\rho>1
.
To sum up,

{\widehat{\bm{m}}}^{\top}{\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}}^{+}{\widehat{\bm{m}}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle i.p.}}{{\longrightarrow}}\left\{\begin{aligned} &\dfrac{\rho}{1-\rho},&\text{for }\rho<1,\\
&\dfrac{1}{\rho-1},&\text{for }\rho>1.\end{aligned}\right.

Limit of
D3
: The challenge in determining the limit of
{\bm{\mu}}^{\top}{\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}}^{+}{\widehat{\bm{m}}}
arise from the fact that the quadratic form involves
{\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}}^{+}
being multiplied by distinct vectors on its left and right sides. Therefore, we examine the square of
D3
,
{\widehat{\bm{m}}}^{\top}{\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}}^{+}{\bm{\mu}}{\bm{\mu}}^{\top}{\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}}^{+}{\widehat{\bm{m}}}
, where the vectors on both the left and right sides of the quadratic form are identical. We have

\displaystyle{\widehat{\bm{m}}}^{\top}{\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}}^{+}{\bm{\mu}}{\bm{\mu}}^{\top}{\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}}^{+}{\widehat{\bm{m}}}=

\displaystyle\left(\dfrac{\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{e}}{T}\right)^{\top}\left(\frac{1}{T}\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{H}\bm{Y}\right)^{+}{\bm{\mu}}{\bm{\mu}}^{\top}\left(\frac{1}{T}\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{H}\bm{Y}\right)^{+}\left(\dfrac{\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{e}}{T}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\dfrac{1}{T}\left(\sqrt{T}{\bar{{\bm{s}}}}\right)^{\top}{\bm{\mathcal{S}}}^{+}{\bm{\mu}}{\bm{\mu}}^{\top}{\bm{\mathcal{S}}}^{+}\left(\sqrt{T}{\bar{{\bm{s}}}}\right).

By adopting this approach, we can apply the same reasoning used in
D2
, where
\sqrt{T}{\bar{{\bm{s}}}}
is considered a vector of
N
random variables, each following a normal distribution
\mathcal{N}(0,1)
. Consequently we have

\dfrac{T}{N}{\widehat{\bm{m}}}^{\top}{\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}}^{+}{\bm{\mu}}{\bm{\mu}}^{\top}{\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}}^{+}{\widehat{\bm{m}}}=\dfrac{1}{N}\left(\sqrt{T}{\bar{{\bm{s}}}}\right)^{\top}{\bm{\mathcal{S}}}^{+}{\bm{\mu}}{\bm{\mu}}^{\top}{\bm{\mathcal{S}}}^{+}\left(\sqrt{T}{\bar{{\bm{s}}}}\right),

such that according to the trace Lemma
1
,

\dfrac{T}{N}{\widehat{\bm{m}}}^{\top}{\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}}^{+}{\bm{\mu}}{\bm{\mu}}^{\top}{\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}}^{+}{\widehat{\bm{m}}}-\dfrac{1}{N}\Tr\left({\bm{\mathcal{S}}}^{+}{\bm{\mu}}{\bm{\mu}}^{\top}{\bm{\mathcal{S}}}^{+}\right)=O_{p}\left(N^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right).

Subsequently,
\Tr\left({\bm{\mathcal{S}}}^{+}{\bm{\mu}}{\bm{\mu}}^{\top}{\bm{\mathcal{S}}}^{+}\right)={\bm{\mu}}^{\top}[{\bm{\mathcal{S}}}^{+}]^{2}{\bm{\mu}}
. Easily we can get

\frac{{\bm{\mu}}^{\top}[{\bm{\mathcal{S}}}^{+}]^{2}{\bm{\mu}}}{{\bm{\mu}}^{\top}\bm{I}_{N}^{-1}{\bm{\mu}}}={\bm{\nu}}^{\top}\left[\left(\frac{1}{T}\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{H}\bm{Y}\right)^{+}\right]^{2}{\bm{\nu}},

where
{\bm{\nu}}={\bm{\mu}}/\left\|{\bm{\mu}}\right\|_{2}
is still a vector of
\ell_{2}
norm 1. By the same argument as the proof for
D1
that the eigenmatrix of sample covariance matrix
{\bm{\mathcal{S}}}
is Haar distributed, we claim that when
\rho<1
,

\frac{1}{N}\left(\sqrt{N}{\bm{\nu}}\right)^{\top}{{\bm{\mathcal{S}}}}^{-2}\left(\sqrt{N}{\bm{\nu}}\right)-\frac{1}{p}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\frac{1}{s_{i}^{2}}=O_{p}\left(N^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right).

And

\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\frac{1}{s_{i}^{2}}=\int\frac{1}{s^{2}}dF_{{\bm{\mathcal{S}}}}(s)\rightarrow\int\frac{1}{s^{2}}dF_{MP,\rho}(s),

where
F_{{\bm{\mathcal{S}}}}
is the spectral measure of
T^{-1}\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{H}\bm{Y}
and
F_{MP,\rho}
is the limiting spectral distribution of
{\bm{\mathcal{S}}}
, the Marčenko Pastur’s law.

Thus, when
\rho<1
,

\frac{{\bm{\mu}}^{\top}[{\bm{\mathcal{S}}}^{+}]^{2}{\bm{\mu}}}{{\bm{\mu}}^{\top}\bm{I}_{N}^{-1}{\bm{\mu}}}\rightarrow\dfrac{1}{(1-\rho)^{3}}.

When
\rho>1
, we claim that

\frac{1}{N}\left(\sqrt{N}{\bm{\nu}}\right)^{\top}{{\bm{\mathcal{S}}}}^{-2}\left(\sqrt{N}{\bm{\nu}}\right)-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{T}\frac{1}{s_{i}^{2}}=O_{p}\left(N^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right).

Again, let
t_{i}
,
i=1,...,T
denote the eigenvalues of
\bm{H}\bm{Y}\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{H}/N
. We write
s_{i}=(N/T)t_{i}
,
i=1,...T
, then we have

\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{T}\frac{1}{s_{i}^{2}}=\left(\frac{T}{N}\right)^{3}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{i=1}^{T}\frac{1}{t_{i}^{2}}=\left(\frac{T}{N}\right)^{3}\int\frac{1}{t^{2}}dF_{\bm{H}\bm{Y}\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{H}/N}(t)\rightarrow\left(\dfrac{1}{\rho}\right)^{3}\int\frac{1}{t^{2}}dF_{MP,1/\rho}(t),

where
F_{\bm{H}\bm{Y}\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{H}/N}
is the spectral measure of
\bm{H}\bm{Y}\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{H}/N
and
F_{MP,1/\rho}
is the LSD. Now by the same arguments as above, we conclude that in probability,

\frac{{\bm{\mu}}^{\top}{\bm{\Sigma}}^{-\frac{1}{2}}[{\bm{\mathcal{S}}}^{+}]^{2}{\bm{\Sigma}}^{-\frac{1}{2}}{\bm{\mu}}}{{\bm{\mu}}^{\top}{\bm{\Sigma}}^{-1}{\bm{\mu}}}\rightarrow\dfrac{1}{(\rho-1)^{3}}.

To sum up,

\frac{{\bm{\mu}}^{\top}[{\bm{\mathcal{S}}}^{+}]^{2}{\bm{\mu}}}{{\bm{\mu}}^{\top}\bm{I}_{N}^{-1}{\bm{\mu}}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle i.p.}}{{\longrightarrow}}\left\{\begin{aligned} &\dfrac{1}{(1-\rho)^{3}},&\text{for }\rho<1,\\
&\dfrac{1}{(\rho-1)^{3}},&\text{for }\rho>1.\end{aligned}\right.

Furthermore, in accordance with Assumption
A3
,

{\bm{\mu}}^{\top}[{\bm{\mathcal{S}}}^{+}]^{2}{\bm{\mu}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle i.p.}}{{\longrightarrow}}\left\{\begin{aligned} &\dfrac{1}{(1-\rho)^{3}}{\tilde{\theta}},&\text{for }\rho<1,\\
&\dfrac{1}{(\rho-1)^{3}}{\tilde{\theta}},&\text{for }\rho>1,\end{aligned}\right.

Therefore, we have

\left\{\begin{aligned} &\dfrac{T}{N}\cdot\left({\widehat{\bm{m}}}^{\top}{\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}}^{+}{\bm{\mu}}\right)^{2}-\dfrac{1}{N}\cdot\dfrac{1}{(1-\rho)^{3}}{\tilde{\theta}}=O_{p}(N^{-\frac{1}{2}}),&\text{for }\rho<1,\\
&\dfrac{T}{N}\cdot\left({\widehat{\bm{m}}}^{\top}{\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}}^{+}{\bm{\mu}}\right)^{2}-\dfrac{1}{N}\cdot\dfrac{1}{(\rho-1)^{3}}{\tilde{\theta}}=O_{p}(N^{-\frac{1}{2}}),&\text{for }\rho>1,\end{aligned}\right.

\Rightarrow\left\{\begin{aligned} &\left({\widehat{\bm{m}}}^{\top}{\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}}^{+}{\bm{\mu}}\right)^{2}-\dfrac{1}{T}\cdot\dfrac{1}{(1-\rho)^{3}}{\tilde{\theta}}=O_{p}(T^{-\frac{1}{2}}),&\text{for }\rho<1,\\
&\left({\widehat{\bm{m}}}^{\top}{\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}}^{+}{\bm{\mu}}\right)^{2}-\dfrac{1}{T}\cdot\dfrac{1}{(\rho-1)^{3}}{\tilde{\theta}}=O_{p}(T^{-\frac{1}{2}}),&\text{for }\rho>1.\end{aligned}\right.

Given that
{\tilde{\theta}}
and
\rho
are constants and bounded, and under Assumption
A1
which assumes
T\rightarrow\infty
, it follows that
{\bm{\mu}}^{\top}{\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}}^{+}{\widehat{\bm{m}}}
converges to 0 for all values of
\rho
.

Limit of
D4
: The proof is similar to that for
D3
. Easily we can get

{\bm{\mu}}^{\top}{\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}}^{+}\bm{I}_{N}{\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}}^{+}{\bm{\mu}}={\bm{\mu}}^{\top}[{\bm{\mathcal{S}}}^{+}]^{2}{\bm{\mu}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle i.p.}}{{\longrightarrow}}\left\{\begin{aligned} &\dfrac{1}{(1-\rho)^{3}}{\tilde{\theta}},&\text{for }\rho<1,\\
&\dfrac{1}{(\rho-1)^{3}}{\tilde{\theta}},&\text{for }\rho>1.\end{aligned}\right.

Limit of
D5
: Again, similar to the statistic
{\widehat{\bm{m}}}^{\top}{\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}}^{+}{\widehat{\bm{m}}}
, the only difference between the limiting behaviors of
{\widehat{\bm{m}}}^{\top}{\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}}^{+}\bm{I}_{N}{\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}}^{+}{\widehat{\bm{m}}}
and
{\bm{\mu}}^{\top}{\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}}^{+}\bm{I}_{N}{\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}}^{+}{\bm{\mu}}/{\bm{\mu}}^{\top}\bm{I}_{N}^{-1}{\bm{\mu}}
lies in the multiplication factor of
\left\|{\bar{{\bm{s}}}}\right\|^{2}
, and we can easily get the following results:

{\widehat{\bm{m}}}^{\top}{\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}}^{+}\bm{I}_{N}{\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}}^{+}{\widehat{\bm{m}}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle i.p.}}{{\longrightarrow}}\left\{\begin{aligned} &\dfrac{\rho}{(1-\rho)^{3}},&\text{for }\rho<1,\\
&\dfrac{\rho}{(\rho-1)^{3}},&\text{for }\rho>1.\end{aligned}\right.

Limit of
D6
:
Mirroring the approach for
D3
,
D6
also presents an imbalance in its quadratic form. To address this, we analyze the square of
D6
, given by
{\widehat{\bm{m}}}^{\top}{\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}}^{+}\bm{I}_{N}{\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}}^{+}{\bm{\mu}}{\bm{\mu}}^{\top}{\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}}^{+}\bm{I}_{N}{\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}}^{+}{\widehat{\bm{m}}}
. This formulation simplifies to:

\displaystyle{\widehat{\bm{m}}}^{\top}{\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}}^{+}\bm{I}_{N}{\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}}^{+}{\bm{\mu}}{\bm{\mu}}^{\top}{\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}}^{+}\bm{I}_{N}{\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}}^{+}{\widehat{\bm{m}}}=

\displaystyle\left(\dfrac{\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{e}}{T}\right)^{\top}[{\bm{\mathcal{S}}}^{+}]^{2}{\bm{\mu}}{\bm{\mu}}^{\top}[{\bm{\mathcal{S}}}^{+}]^{2}\left(\dfrac{\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{e}}{T}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\dfrac{1}{T}\left(\sqrt{T}{\bar{{\bm{s}}}}\right)^{\top}[{\bm{\mathcal{S}}}^{+}]^{2}{\bm{\mu}}{\bm{\mu}}^{\top}[{\bm{\mathcal{S}}}^{+}]^{2}\left(\sqrt{T}{\bar{{\bm{s}}}}\right).

Using the same rationale as in
D3
and invoking the Trace Lemma
1
, we deduce that

\dfrac{T}{N}{\widehat{\bm{m}}}^{\top}{\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}}^{+}\bm{I}_{N}{\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}}^{+}{\bm{\mu}}{\bm{\mu}}^{\top}{\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}}^{+}\bm{I}_{N}{\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}}^{+}{\widehat{\bm{m}}}-\dfrac{1}{N}\Tr\left([{\bm{\mathcal{S}}}^{+}]^{2}{\bm{\mu}}{\bm{\mu}}^{\top}[{\bm{\mathcal{S}}}^{+}]^{2}\right)=O_{p}\left(N^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right).

Furthermore,
\Tr\left([{\bm{\mathcal{S}}}^{+}]^{2}{\bm{\mu}}{\bm{\mu}}^{\top}[{\bm{\mathcal{S}}}^{+}]^{2}\right)={\bm{\mu}}^{\top}[{\bm{\mathcal{S}}}^{+}]^{4}{\bm{\mu}}
. Similar to
D4
, we examine the ratio of
{\bm{\mu}}^{\top}[{\bm{\mathcal{S}}}^{+}]^{4}{\bm{\mu}}
relative to
{\bm{\mu}}^{\top}\bm{I}_{N}^{-1}{\bm{\mu}}
. We have

\frac{{\bm{\mu}}^{\top}[{\bm{\mathcal{S}}}^{+}]^{4}{\bm{\mu}}}{{\bm{\mu}}^{\top}\bm{I}_{N}^{-1}{\bm{\mu}}}={\bm{\nu}}^{\top}[{\bm{\mathcal{S}}}^{+}]^{4}{\bm{\nu}},

where
{\bm{\nu}}={\bm{\mu}}/\left\|{\bm{\mu}}\right\|_{2}
is a vector of
\ell_{2}
norm 1. By the same argument as the proof for
D1
that the eigenmatrix of sample covariance matrix
{\bm{\mathcal{S}}}
is Haar distributed, we claim that when
\rho<1
,

\dfrac{1}{N}\left(\sqrt{N}{\bm{\nu}}\right)^{\top}[{\bm{\mathcal{S}}}^{+}]^{4}\left(\sqrt{N}{\bm{\nu}}\right)-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\frac{1}{s_{i}^{4}}=O_{p}\left(N^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right),

and

\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\frac{1}{s_{i}^{4}}=\int\frac{1}{s^{4}}dF_{{\bm{\mathcal{S}}}}(s)\rightarrow\int\frac{1}{s^{4}}dF_{MP,\rho}(s).

Thus, when
\rho<1
,

\frac{{\bm{\mu}}^{\top}[{\bm{\mathcal{S}}}^{+}]^{4}{\bm{\mu}}}{{\bm{\mu}}^{\top}\bm{I}_{N}^{-1}{\bm{\mu}}}\rightarrow\int\frac{1}{s^{4}}dF_{MP,\rho}(s).

When
\rho>1
, we claim that

\dfrac{1}{N}\left(\sqrt{N}{\bm{\nu}}\right)^{\top}[{\bm{\mathcal{S}}}^{+}]^{4}\left(\sqrt{N}{\bm{\nu}}\right)-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{T}\frac{1}{s_{i}^{4}}=O_{p}\left(N^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right),

Again, let
t_{i}
,
i=1,...,T
denote the eigenvalues of
\bm{H}\bm{Y}\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{H}/N
. We write
s_{i}=(N/T)t_{i}
,
i=1,...T
, then we have

\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{T}\frac{1}{s_{i}^{4}}=\left(\frac{T}{N}\right)^{5}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{i=1}^{T}\frac{1}{t_{i}^{4}}=\left(\frac{T}{N}\right)^{5}\int\frac{1}{t^{4}}dF_{\bm{H}\bm{Y}\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{H}/N}(t)\rightarrow\left(\dfrac{1}{\rho}\right)^{5}\int\frac{1}{t^{4}}dF_{MP,1/\rho}(t).

Now by the same arguments as above, we conclude that in probability,

\frac{{\bm{\mu}}^{\top}[{\bm{\mathcal{S}}}^{+}]^{4}{\bm{\mu}}}{{\bm{\mu}}^{\top}\bm{I}_{N}^{-1}{\bm{\mu}}}\rightarrow\dfrac{1}{\rho^{5}}\int\frac{1}{t^{4}}dF_{MP,1/\rho}(t).

To sum up,

\frac{{\bm{\mu}}^{\top}[{\bm{\mathcal{S}}}^{+}]^{4}{\bm{\mu}}}{{\bm{\mu}}^{\top}\bm{I}_{N}^{-1}{\bm{\mu}}}\rightarrow\left\{\begin{aligned} &\int\frac{1}{s^{4}}dF_{MP,\rho}(s),&\text{for }\rho<1,\\
&\dfrac{1}{\rho^{5}}\int\frac{1}{t^{4}}dF_{MP,1/\rho}(t),&\text{for }\rho>1.\end{aligned}\right.

Finally, we have

\left\{\begin{aligned} &\dfrac{T}{N}\cdot\left({\widehat{\bm{m}}}^{\top}{\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}}^{+}\bm{I}_{N}{\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}}^{+}{\bm{\mu}}\right)^{2}-\dfrac{1}{N}\cdot\int\frac{1}{s^{4}}dF_{MP,\rho}(s)\cdot{\tilde{\theta}}=O_{p}(N^{-\frac{1}{2}}),&\text{for }\rho<1,\\
&\dfrac{T}{N}\cdot\left({\widehat{\bm{m}}}^{\top}{\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}}^{+}\bm{I}_{N}{\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}}^{+}{\bm{\mu}}\right)^{2}-\dfrac{1}{N}\cdot\dfrac{1}{\rho^{5}}\int\frac{1}{t^{4}}dF_{MP,1/\rho}(t)\cdot{\tilde{\theta}}=O_{p}(N^{-\frac{1}{2}}),&\text{for }\rho>1,\end{aligned}\right.

\Rightarrow\left\{\begin{aligned} &\left({\widehat{\bm{m}}}^{\top}{\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}}^{+}\bm{I}_{N}{\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}}^{+}{\bm{\mu}}\right)^{2}-\dfrac{1}{T}\cdot\int\frac{1}{s^{4}}dF_{MP,\rho}(s)\cdot{\tilde{\theta}}=O_{p}(T^{-\frac{1}{2}}),&\text{for }\rho<1,\\
&\left({\widehat{\bm{m}}}^{\top}{\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}}^{+}\bm{I}_{N}{\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}}^{+}{\bm{\mu}}\right)^{2}-\dfrac{1}{T}\cdot\dfrac{1}{\rho^{5}}\int\frac{1}{t^{4}}dF_{MP,1/\rho}(t)\cdot{\tilde{\theta}}=O_{p}(T^{-\frac{1}{2}}),&\text{for }\rho>1.\end{aligned}\right.

Given that
{\tilde{\theta}}
,
\rho
,
\int\frac{1}{s^{4}}dF_{MP,\rho}(s)
and
\int\frac{1}{t^{4}}dF_{MP,1/\rho}(t)
are bounded constants, and under Assumption
A1
which assumes
T\rightarrow\infty
, it follows that
{\bm{\mu}}^{\top}{\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}}^{+}\bm{I}_{N}{\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}}^{+}{\widehat{\bm{m}}}
converges to 0 for all values of
\rho
.

Now it is easy to get the asymptotic limit of
{\widehat{\theta}}_{s}
:

{\widehat{\theta}}_{s}=D1+D2+2D3\stackrel{{\scriptstyle i.p.}}{{\longrightarrow}}\left\{\begin{aligned} &\dfrac{{\tilde{\theta}}+\rho}{1-\rho},&\text{for }\rho<1,\\
&\dfrac{{\tilde{\theta}}+\rho}{\rho(\rho-1)},&\text{for }\rho>1.\end{aligned}\right.

By rearrangement, we have the resulst in Theorem
1
. Also, We can express
L_{{\bm{R}}}\left[\widehat{{\bm{\omega}}^{\ast}_{{}^{+}}};{{\bm{\omega}^{\ast}}}\right]
in terms of
D1
to
D6
. The formulation is given by:

\displaystyle L_{{\bm{R}}}\left[\widehat{{\bm{\omega}}^{\ast}_{{}^{+}}};{{\bm{\omega}^{\ast}}}\right]

\displaystyle=\sigma^{2}\left[\dfrac{D4+D5+2D6}{{\widehat{\theta}}}-2\dfrac{D1+D3}{\sqrt{{\widehat{\theta}}}\sqrt{\theta}}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\dfrac{\left(D1+D3\right)^{2}}{{\widehat{\theta}}}-2\dfrac{\left(D1+D3\right)\sqrt{\theta}}{\sqrt{{\widehat{\theta}}}}+1+\theta\right].

By substituting the above limiting results, it is easy to get the conclusion in Theorem
2
.
Similarly, the Sharpe ratio can be expressed using
D1
to
D6
:

\displaystyle SR\left[\widehat{{\bm{\omega}}^{\ast}_{{}^{+}}}\right]=\dfrac{D1+D3}{\sqrt{D4+D5+2D6}}.

Here finishes the proof for Theorem
1
,
1
,
2
.
∎

C.3
Proofs for Proposition
2
, Theorem
3
and Theorem
4

Proof:
.

Based on the DGP in Assumption
B2
, we can formulate single-factor stock returns as:

\bm{r}_{t}={\bm{b}}\mu_{f}+{\bm{b}}\sigma_{f}\cdot z_{t}+\sigma_{\epsilon}\cdot{\bm{y}}_{t},\quad 1\leq t\leq T,

or in matrix form:

{\bm{R}}=\mu_{f}\cdot\bm{e}{\bm{b}}^{\top}+\sigma_{f}{\bm{z}}{\bm{b}}^{\top}+\sigma_{\epsilon}\bm{Y},

where
z_{t}
and
{\bm{y}}_{t}
are independent,
{\bm{z}}
is a
T\times 1
vector of
i.i.d.
random variables with
\mathbb{E}[z_{1}]=0
,
\mathbb{E}[z_{1}]^{2}=1
,
\mathbb{E}[z_{1}]^{4}<\infty
,
\bm{Y}
is a
T\times N
matrix of
i.i.d.
random variables with
\mathbb{E}[y_{11}]=0
,
\mathbb{E}[y_{11}]^{2}=1
,
\mathbb{E}[y_{11}]^{4}<\infty
and
\bm{e}
is a vector of ones. The proof becomes more straightforward when we formulated in matrices.

Again, let
{\widehat{\bm{m}}}={\widehat{{\bm{\mu}}}}-{\bm{\mu}}
represent the centered sample mean vector. Then under single factor model,

{\widehat{\bm{m}}}=\frac{\sigma_{f}{\bm{b}}{\bm{z}}^{\top}\bm{e}}{T}+\frac{\sigma_{\epsilon}\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{e}}{T}.

Recall
\bm{H}=\bm{I}-\frac{\bm{e}\bm{e}^{\top}}{T}
and the difference between
1/T
and
1/(T-1)
becomes negligible when considering the limit as
T\rightarrow\infty
, we consider

\displaystyle{\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}}

\displaystyle=\dfrac{1}{T}\left({\bm{R}}-\bar{{\bm{R}}}\right)^{\top}\left({\bm{R}}-\bar{{\bm{R}}}\right)

\displaystyle=\dfrac{1}{T}\left(\bm{H}{\bm{R}}\right)^{\top}\left(\bm{H}{\bm{R}}\right)

\displaystyle=\dfrac{1}{T}\left(\sigma_{f}\bm{H}{\bm{z}}{\bm{b}}^{\top}+\bm{H}\bm{Y}\sigma_{\epsilon}\right)^{\top}\left(\sigma_{f}\bm{H}{\bm{z}}{\bm{b}}^{\top}+\bm{H}\bm{Y}\sigma_{\epsilon}\right)

\displaystyle=\sigma_{\epsilon}^{2}\left(\dfrac{1}{T}\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{H}\bm{Y}\right)+\sigma_{f}\sigma_{\epsilon}\left(\dfrac{1}{T}\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{H}{\bm{z}}\right){\bm{b}}^{\top}

\displaystyle\quad+\sigma_{f}\sigma_{\epsilon}{\bm{b}}\left(\dfrac{1}{T}\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{H}{\bm{z}}\right)^{\top}+\sigma_{f}^{2}{\bm{b}}{\bm{b}}^{\top}\left(\dfrac{1}{T}{\bm{z}}^{\top}\bm{H}{\bm{z}}\right).

Let us infer
\frac{1}{T}{\bm{z}}^{\top}\bm{H}{\bm{z}}
first. We know that
\frac{1}{T}{\bm{z}}^{\top}{\bm{z}}=\frac{1}{T}\left(z_{1}^{2}+\cdots+z_{T}^{2}\right)
, and it is easy to get
\mathbb{E}[\frac{1}{T}{\bm{z}}^{\top}{\bm{z}}]=1
and
\Var[\frac{1}{T}{\bm{z}}^{\top}{\bm{z}}]=\frac{1}{T}\left(\mathbb{E}[z_{1}]^{4}-1\right)
. As we assume
\mathbb{E}[z_{1}]^{4}
is bounded, we have
\frac{1}{T}{\bm{z}}^{\top}{\bm{z}}=1+O_{p}(T^{-\frac{1}{2}})
. Also
\frac{{\bm{z}}^{\top}\bm{e}}{\sqrt{T}}
follows
\mathcal{N}(0,1)
,
\left(\frac{{\bm{z}}^{\top}\bm{e}}{\sqrt{T}}\right)^{2}
follows
\chi^{2}(1)
, we have
\left(\frac{{\bm{z}}^{\top}\bm{e}}{T}\right)^{2}=O_{p}(T^{-1})
. Thus,
\frac{1}{T}{\bm{z}}^{\top}\bm{H}{\bm{z}}=1+O_{p}(T^{-\frac{1}{2}})
.

Denote
{\bm{\mathcal{S}}}=\frac{1}{T}\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{H}\bm{Y}
, we consider

\displaystyle{\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}}=\sigma_{\epsilon}^{2}{\bm{\mathcal{S}}}+\sigma_{f}\sigma_{\epsilon}\left(\dfrac{1}{T}\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{H}{\bm{z}}\right){\bm{b}}^{\top}+\sigma_{f}\sigma_{\epsilon}{\bm{b}}\left(\dfrac{1}{T}\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{H}{\bm{z}}\right)^{\top}+\sigma_{f}^{2}{\bm{b}}{\bm{b}}^{\top}.

In the following, we prove for the case
\rho<1
and
\rho>1
separately. Similar to the proof of Theorem
1
,
1
and
2
, we will determine the limits of 6 terms. They are
E1={\bm{\mu}}^{\top}{\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}}^{-1}{\bm{\mu}}
,
E2={\widehat{\bm{m}}}^{\top}{\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}}^{-1}{\widehat{\bm{m}}}
,
E3={\bm{\mu}}^{\top}{\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}}^{-1}{\widehat{\bm{m}}}
,

E4={\bm{\mu}}^{\top}{\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}}^{-1}{\bm{\Sigma}}{\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}}^{-1}{\bm{\mu}}
,
E5={\widehat{\bm{m}}}^{\top}{\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}}^{-1}{\bm{\Sigma}}{\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}}^{-1}{\widehat{\bm{m}}}
,

E6={\bm{\mu}}^{\top}{\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}}^{-1}{\bm{\Sigma}}{\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}}^{-1}{\widehat{\bm{m}}}
.

C.3.1
Case
\rho<1

Before proving, we introduce the Sherman–Morrison formula. For any invertible matrix
{\bm{M}}
, vectors
{\bm{m}}
,
{\bm{n}}
and a scalar
q
,

\left({\bm{M}}+q{\bm{m}}{\bm{n}}^{\top}\right)^{-1}={\bm{M}}^{-1}-\frac{q{\bm{M}}^{-1}{\bm{m}}{\bm{n}}^{\top}{\bm{M}}^{-1}}{1+q{\bm{n}}^{\top}{\bm{M}}^{-1}{\bm{m}}}.

In this sub-section, we consider

\displaystyle{\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}}=\sigma_{\epsilon}^{2}{\bm{\mathcal{S}}}+\left(\sigma_{f}\sigma_{\epsilon}\dfrac{1}{T}\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{H}{\bm{z}}\right){\bm{b}}^{\top}+{\bm{b}}\left(\sigma_{f}\sigma_{\epsilon}\dfrac{1}{T}\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{H}{\bm{z}}\right)^{\top}+\sigma_{f}^{2}{\bm{b}}{\bm{b}}^{\top}.

Denote

\displaystyle{\bm{C}}

\displaystyle=\sigma_{\epsilon}^{2}{\bm{\mathcal{S}}},

\displaystyle{\bm{B}}

\displaystyle={\bm{C}}+\left(\sigma_{f}\sigma_{\epsilon}\frac{1}{T}\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{H}{\bm{z}}\right){\bm{b}}^{\top},

\displaystyle{\bm{A}}

\displaystyle={\bm{B}}+{\bm{b}}\left(\sigma_{f}\sigma_{\epsilon}\dfrac{1}{T}\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{H}{\bm{z}}\right)^{\top},

then using the above Sherman–Morrison formula, we have

\displaystyle{\bm{C}}^{-1}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sigma_{\epsilon}^{2}}{\bm{\mathcal{S}}}^{-1},

\displaystyle{\bm{B}}^{-1}

\displaystyle={\bm{C}}^{-1}-\frac{\frac{\sigma_{f}}{\sigma_{\epsilon}^{3}}{\bm{\mathcal{S}}}^{-1}\left(\frac{1}{T}\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{H}{\bm{z}}\right){\bm{b}}^{\top}{\bm{\mathcal{S}}}^{-1}}{1+\frac{\sigma_{f}}{\sigma_{\epsilon}}{\bm{b}}^{\top}{\bm{\mathcal{S}}}^{-1}\frac{1}{T}\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{H}{\bm{z}}},

\displaystyle{\bm{A}}^{-1}

\displaystyle={\bm{B}}^{-1}-\frac{\sigma_{f}\sigma_{\epsilon}{\bm{B}}^{-1}{\bm{b}}\left(\frac{1}{T}\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{H}{\bm{z}}\right)^{\top}{\bm{B}}^{-1}}{1+\sigma_{f}\sigma_{\epsilon}\left(\frac{1}{T}\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{H}{\bm{z}}\right)^{\top}{\bm{B}}^{-1}{\bm{b}}},

\displaystyle{\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}}^{-1}

\displaystyle={\bm{A}}^{-1}-\frac{\sigma_{f}^{2}{\bm{A}}^{-1}{\bm{b}}{\bm{b}}^{\top}{\bm{A}}^{-1}}{1+\sigma_{f}^{2}{\bm{b}}^{\top}{\bm{A}}^{-1}{\bm{b}}}.

We organize the following into three parts: First, in
C.3.1.1
, we present the asymptotic results for
E1
-
E6
. Second, in
C.3.1.2
, we list the asymptotic results for the intermediate terms that appear in
C.3.1.1
. Third, in
C.3.1.3
, we provide the proofs for the asymptotic results of the intermediate terms. Also, recall that
\phi=\frac{\sigma_{f}^{2}\left\|{\bm{b}}\right\|^{2}}{\sigma_{\epsilon}^{2}}
.

C.3.1.1
Asymptotic results of
E1
-
E6

Limit of
E1
: We have

\displaystyle E1

\displaystyle={\bm{\mu}}^{\top}{\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}}^{-1}{\bm{\mu}}

\displaystyle=\mu_{f}^{2}{\bm{b}}^{\top}{\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}}^{-1}{\bm{b}}

\displaystyle=\mu_{f}^{2}\gamma_{{\widehat{\Sigma}}1}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle i.p.}}{{\longrightarrow}}\frac{\mu_{f}^{2}\frac{1}{\sigma_{\epsilon}^{2}}c}{1-\frac{\sigma_{f}^{2}}{\sigma_{\epsilon}^{2}}\rho c+\frac{\sigma_{f}^{2}}{\sigma_{\epsilon}^{2}}c}=\frac{{\tilde{\theta}}}{1-\rho}.

Limit of
E2
: We have

\displaystyle E2

\displaystyle={\widehat{\bm{m}}}^{\top}{\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}}^{-1}{\widehat{\bm{m}}}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{\sigma_{f}{\bm{b}}{\bm{z}}^{\top}\bm{e}}{T}+\frac{\sigma_{\epsilon}\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{e}}{T}\right)^{\top}{\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}}^{-1}\left(\frac{\sigma_{f}{\bm{b}}{\bm{z}}^{\top}\bm{e}}{T}+\frac{\sigma_{\epsilon}\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{e}}{T}\right)

\displaystyle=E21+E22+2E23,

where

\displaystyle E21

\displaystyle=\sigma_{f}^{2}\left(\frac{{\bm{z}}^{\top}\bm{e}}{\sqrt{T}}\right)^{2}\frac{1}{T}{\bm{b}}^{\top}{\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}}^{-1}{\bm{b}}=\sigma_{f}^{2}\left(\frac{{\bm{z}}^{\top}\bm{e}}{\sqrt{T}}\right)^{2}\frac{1}{T}\gamma_{{\widehat{\Sigma}}1}=O_{p}(T^{-1}),

\displaystyle E22

\displaystyle=\sigma_{\epsilon}^{2}{\tilde{\tilde{\alpha}}}_{{\widehat{\Sigma}}1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle i.p.}}{{\longrightarrow}}\frac{\rho}{1-\rho},

\displaystyle E23

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\sigma_{f}\sigma_{\epsilon}\left(\frac{{\bm{z}}^{\top}\bm{e}}{\sqrt{T}}\right){\tilde{\beta}}_{{\widehat{\Sigma}}1}=O_{p}(T^{-1}).

Thus,
E2\stackrel{{\scriptstyle i.p.}}{{\longrightarrow}}\frac{\rho}{1-\rho}
.

Limit of
E3
: We have

\displaystyle E3

\displaystyle={\bm{\mu}}^{\top}{\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}}^{-1}{\widehat{\bm{m}}}

\displaystyle=\mu_{f}{\bm{b}}^{\top}{\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}}^{-1}\left(\frac{\sigma_{f}{\bm{b}}{\bm{z}}^{\top}\bm{e}}{T}+\frac{\sigma_{\epsilon}\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{e}}{T}\right)

\displaystyle=E31+E32,

where

\displaystyle E31

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\mu_{f}\sigma_{f}\left(\frac{{\bm{z}}^{\top}\bm{e}}{\sqrt{T}}\right){\bm{b}}^{\top}{\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}}^{-1}{\bm{b}}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\mu_{f}\sigma_{f}\left(\frac{{\bm{z}}^{\top}\bm{e}}{\sqrt{T}}\right)\gamma_{{\widehat{\Sigma}}1}=O_{p}(T^{-\frac{1}{2}}),

\displaystyle E32

\displaystyle=\mu_{f}\sigma_{\epsilon}{\tilde{\beta}}_{{\widehat{\Sigma}}1}=O_{p}(T^{-\frac{1}{2}}).

Thus,
E3=O_{p}(T^{-\frac{1}{2}})
.

Limit of
E4
: We have

\displaystyle E4

\displaystyle={\bm{\mu}}^{\top}{\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}}^{-1}{\bm{\Sigma}}{\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}}^{-1}{\bm{\mu}}

\displaystyle=\mu_{f}^{2}{\bm{b}}^{\top}{\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}}^{-1}\left(\sigma_{f}^{2}{\bm{b}}{\bm{b}}^{\top}+\sigma_{\epsilon}^{2}\bm{I}_{N}\right){\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}}^{-1}{\bm{b}}

\displaystyle=\mu_{f}^{2}\sigma_{f}^{2}\left({\bm{b}}^{\top}{\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}}^{-1}{\bm{b}}\right)^{2}+\mu_{f}^{2}\sigma_{\epsilon}^{2}\left({\bm{b}}^{\top}{\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}}^{-1}{\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}}^{-1}{\bm{b}}\right)

\displaystyle=\mu_{f}^{2}\sigma_{f}^{2}\gamma_{{\widehat{\Sigma}}1}^{2}+\mu_{f}^{2}\sigma_{\epsilon}^{2}\gamma_{{\widehat{\Sigma}}2}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle i.p.}}{{\longrightarrow}}\frac{\frac{\mu_{f}^{2}}{\sigma_{\epsilon}^{2}}\tilde{b}^{2}\frac{1}{(1-\rho)^{3}}\left[1+\frac{\sigma_{f}^{2}}{\sigma_{\epsilon}^{2}}\tilde{b}^{2}\right]}{\left(1-\frac{\sigma_{f}^{2}}{\sigma_{\epsilon}^{2}}\rho c+\frac{\sigma_{f}^{2}}{\sigma_{\epsilon}^{2}}c\right)^{2}}=\frac{{\tilde{\theta}}}{(1-\rho)^{3}}.

Limit of
E5
: We have

\displaystyle E5

\displaystyle={\widehat{\bm{m}}}^{\top}{\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}}^{-1}{\bm{\Sigma}}{\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}}^{-1}{\widehat{\bm{m}}}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{\sigma_{f}{\bm{b}}{\bm{z}}^{\top}\bm{e}}{T}+\frac{\sigma_{\epsilon}\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{e}}{T}\right)^{\top}{\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}}^{-1}\left(\sigma_{f}^{2}{\bm{b}}{\bm{b}}^{\top}+\sigma_{\epsilon}^{2}\bm{I}_{N}\right){\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}}^{-1}\left(\frac{\sigma_{f}{\bm{b}}{\bm{z}}^{\top}\bm{e}}{T}+\frac{\sigma_{\epsilon}\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{e}}{T}\right)

\displaystyle=E51+E52+2E53+E54+E55+2E56,

where

\displaystyle E51

\displaystyle=\sigma_{f}^{4}\left(\frac{{\bm{z}}^{\top}\bm{e}}{\sqrt{T}}\right)^{2}\frac{1}{T}\left({\bm{b}}^{\top}{\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}}^{-1}{\bm{b}}\right)^{2}=\sigma_{f}^{4}\left(\frac{{\bm{z}}^{\top}\bm{e}}{\sqrt{T}}\right)^{2}\frac{1}{T}\gamma_{{\widehat{\Sigma}}1}^{2}=O_{p}(T^{-1}),

\displaystyle E52

\displaystyle=\sigma_{f}^{2}\sigma_{\epsilon}^{2}{\tilde{\beta}}_{{\widehat{\Sigma}}1}^{2}=O_{p}(T^{-1}),

\displaystyle E53

\displaystyle=\sigma_{f}^{3}\sigma_{\epsilon}\left(\frac{{\bm{z}}^{\top}\bm{e}}{\sqrt{T}}\right)\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\gamma_{{\widehat{\Sigma}}1}{\tilde{\beta}}_{{\widehat{\Sigma}}1}=O_{p}(T^{-1}),

\displaystyle E54

\displaystyle=\sigma_{f}^{2}\sigma_{\epsilon}^{2}\left(\frac{{\bm{z}}^{\top}\bm{e}}{\sqrt{T}}\right)^{2}\frac{1}{T}\gamma_{{\widehat{\Sigma}}2}=O_{p}(T^{-1})

\displaystyle E55

\displaystyle=\sigma_{\epsilon}^{4}{\tilde{\tilde{\alpha}}}_{{\widehat{\Sigma}}2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle i.p.}}{{\longrightarrow}}\frac{\rho}{(1-\rho)^{3}},

\displaystyle E56

\displaystyle=\sigma_{f}\sigma_{\epsilon}^{3}\left(\frac{{\bm{z}}^{\top}\bm{e}}{\sqrt{T}}\right)\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}{\tilde{\beta}}_{{\widehat{\Sigma}}2}=O_{p}(T^{-1})

(C.6)

Thus,
E5\stackrel{{\scriptstyle i.p.}}{{\longrightarrow}}\frac{\rho}{(1-\rho)^{3}}
.

Limit of
E6
: We have

\displaystyle E6

\displaystyle={\bm{\mu}}^{\top}{\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}}^{-1}{\bm{\Sigma}}{\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}}^{-1}{\widehat{\bm{m}}}

\displaystyle=\mu_{f}{\bm{b}}^{\top}{\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}}^{-1}\left(\sigma_{f}^{2}{\bm{b}}{\bm{b}}^{\top}+\sigma_{\epsilon}^{2}\bm{I}_{N}\right){\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}}^{-1}\left(\frac{\sigma_{f}{\bm{b}}{\bm{z}}^{\top}\bm{e}}{T}+\frac{\sigma_{\epsilon}\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{e}}{T}\right)

\displaystyle=E61+E62+E63+E64,

where

\displaystyle E61

\displaystyle=\mu_{f}\sigma_{f}^{3}\left(\frac{{\bm{z}}^{\top}\bm{e}}{\sqrt{T}}\right)\left({\bm{b}}^{\top}{\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}}^{-1}{\bm{b}}\right)^{2}\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}=\mu_{f}\sigma_{f}^{3}\left(\frac{{\bm{z}}^{\top}\bm{e}}{\sqrt{T}}\right)\gamma_{{\widehat{\Sigma}}1}^{2}\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}=O_{p}(T^{-\frac{1}{2}}),

\displaystyle E62

\displaystyle=\mu_{f}\sigma_{\epsilon}\sigma_{f}^{2}\gamma_{{\widehat{\Sigma}}1}{\tilde{\beta}}_{{\widehat{\Sigma}}1}=O_{p}(T^{-\frac{1}{2}}),

\displaystyle E63

\displaystyle=\mu_{f}\sigma_{\epsilon}^{2}\sigma_{f}\gamma_{{\widehat{\Sigma}}2}\left(\frac{{\bm{z}}^{\top}\bm{e}}{\sqrt{T}}\right)\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}=O_{p}(T^{-\frac{1}{2}}),

\displaystyle E64

\displaystyle=\mu_{f}\sigma_{\epsilon}^{3}{\tilde{\beta}}_{{\widehat{\Sigma}}2}=O_{p}(T^{-\frac{1}{2}}).

Thus,
E6=O_{p}(T^{-\frac{1}{2}})
.

C.3.1.3
Proofs for
C.3.1.2

Limit of
\gamma_{S1}
: By the same justification in
D1
, we have

\gamma_{S1}={\bm{b}}^{\top}{\bm{\mathcal{S}}}^{-1}{\bm{b}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle i.p.}}{{\longrightarrow}}\frac{\tilde{b}^{2}}{1-\rho}=c.

Limit of
\beta_{S1}
: Consider the square of
\beta_{S1}
, we have

T\beta_{S1}^{2}=\frac{1}{T}{\bm{z}}^{\top}\bm{H}\bm{Y}{\bm{\mathcal{S}}}^{-1}{\bm{b}}{\bm{b}}^{\top}{\bm{\mathcal{S}}}^{-1}\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{H}{\bm{z}}.

Based on trace Lemma
1
, we have

\displaystyle T\beta_{S1}^{2}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{T}{\bm{b}}^{\top}{\bm{\mathcal{S}}}^{-1}\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{H}\bm{Y}{\bm{\mathcal{S}}}^{-1}{\bm{b}}=O_{p}(T^{-\frac{1}{2}}),

\displaystyle T\beta_{S1}^{2}

\displaystyle-{\bm{b}}^{\top}{\bm{\mathcal{S}}}^{-1}{\bm{b}}=O_{p}(T^{-\frac{1}{2}}).

Thus,
\beta_{S1}=O_{p}(T^{-\frac{1}{2}})
.

Limit of
\alpha_{S1}
: Based on trace Lemma
1
, we have

\displaystyle\alpha_{S1}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{T}\Tr\left[\frac{1}{T}\bm{H}\bm{Y}{\bm{\mathcal{S}}}^{-1}\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{H}\right]=O_{p}(T^{-\frac{1}{2}})

\displaystyle\alpha_{S1}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{T}\Tr\left[{\bm{\mathcal{S}}}^{-1}\frac{1}{T}\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{H}\bm{Y}\right]=O_{p}(T^{-\frac{1}{2}})

\displaystyle\alpha_{S1}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{T}\Tr\left[\bm{I}_{N}\right]=O_{p}(T^{-\frac{1}{2}}).

Thus,
\alpha_{S1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle i.p.}}{{\longrightarrow}}\rho
.

Limit of
\beta_{B1}
:

\displaystyle\beta_{B1}

\displaystyle=\left[\frac{1}{T}\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{H}{\bm{z}}\right]^{\top}{\bm{B}}^{-1}{\bm{b}}

\displaystyle=\left[\frac{1}{T}\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{H}{\bm{z}}\right]^{\top}\left[{\bm{C}}^{-1}-\frac{\frac{\sigma_{f}}{\sigma_{\epsilon}^{3}}{\bm{\mathcal{S}}}^{-1}\left(\frac{1}{T}\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{H}{\bm{z}}\right){\bm{b}}^{\top}{\bm{\mathcal{S}}}^{-1}}{1+\frac{\sigma_{f}}{\sigma_{\epsilon}}{\bm{b}}^{\top}{\bm{\mathcal{S}}}^{-1}\frac{1}{T}\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{H}{\bm{z}}}\right]{\bm{b}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sigma_{\epsilon}^{2}}\beta_{S1}-\frac{\frac{\sigma_{f}}{\sigma_{\epsilon}^{3}}\alpha_{S1}\gamma_{S1}}{1+\frac{\sigma_{f}}{\sigma_{\epsilon}}\beta_{S1}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle i.p.}}{{\longrightarrow}}-\frac{\sigma_{f}}{\sigma_{\epsilon}^{3}}\rho c.

Limit of
\gamma_{B1}
:

\displaystyle\gamma_{B1}

\displaystyle={\bm{b}}^{\top}{\bm{B}}^{-1}{\bm{b}}

\displaystyle={\bm{b}}^{\top}\left[{\bm{C}}^{-1}-\frac{\frac{\sigma_{f}}{\sigma_{\epsilon}^{3}}{\bm{\mathcal{S}}}^{-1}\left(\frac{1}{T}\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{H}{\bm{z}}\right){\bm{b}}^{\top}{\bm{\mathcal{S}}}^{-1}}{1+\frac{\sigma_{f}}{\sigma_{\epsilon}}{\bm{b}}^{\top}{\bm{\mathcal{S}}}^{-1}\frac{1}{T}\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{H}{\bm{z}}}\right]{\bm{b}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sigma_{\epsilon}^{2}}\gamma_{S1}-\frac{\frac{\sigma_{f}}{\sigma_{\epsilon}^{3}}\beta_{S1}\gamma_{S1}}{1+\frac{\sigma_{f}}{\sigma_{\epsilon}}\beta_{S1}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle i.p.}}{{\longrightarrow}}\frac{1}{\sigma_{\epsilon}^{2}}c.

Limit of
\gamma_{A1}
:

\displaystyle\gamma_{A1}

\displaystyle={\bm{b}}^{\top}{\bm{A}}^{-1}{\bm{b}}

\displaystyle={\bm{b}}^{\top}\left[{\bm{B}}^{-1}-\frac{\sigma_{f}\sigma_{\epsilon}{\bm{B}}^{-1}{\bm{b}}\left(\frac{1}{T}\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{H}{\bm{z}}\right)^{\top}{\bm{B}}^{-1}}{1+\sigma_{f}\sigma_{\epsilon}\left(\frac{1}{T}\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{H}{\bm{z}}\right)^{\top}{\bm{B}}^{-1}{\bm{b}}}\right]{\bm{b}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\gamma_{B1}}{1+\sigma_{f}\sigma_{\epsilon}\beta_{B1}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle i.p.}}{{\longrightarrow}}\frac{c}{\sigma_{\epsilon}^{2}-\sigma_{f}^{2}\rho c}.

Limit of
\gamma_{{\widehat{\Sigma}}1}
:

\displaystyle\gamma_{{\widehat{\Sigma}}1}

\displaystyle={\bm{b}}^{\top}{\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}}^{-1}{\bm{b}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\gamma_{A1}}{1+\sigma_{f}^{2}\gamma_{A1}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle i.p.}}{{\longrightarrow}}\frac{c}{\sigma_{\epsilon}^{2}-\sigma_{f}^{2}\rho c+\sigma_{f}^{2}c}.

Limit of
{\tilde{\beta}}_{S1}
: By the same justification in

, we have

: By the same justification in

, we have

Limit of
{\tilde{\alpha}}_{S1}

1
, we have

\displaystyle T{\tilde{\alpha}}_{S1}^{2}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{T}\bm{e}^{\top}\bm{Y}{\bm{\mathcal{S}}}^{-1}\frac{1}{T}\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{H}\bm{Y}\frac{1}{T}{\bm{\mathcal{S}}}^{-1}\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{e}=O_{p}(T^{-\frac{1}{2}}),

\displaystyle-{\tilde{\tilde{\alpha}}}_{S1}=O_{p}(T^{-\frac{1}{2}}).

.

{\tilde{\tilde{\alpha}}}_{B1}
:

{\tilde{\beta}}_{A1}

\displaystyle{\tilde{\beta}}_{A1}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{1}{T}\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{e}\right)^{\top}{\bm{A}}^{-1}{\bm{b}}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{1}{T}\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{e}\right)^{\top}\left[{\bm{B}}^{-1}-\frac{\sigma_{f}\sigma_{\epsilon}{\bm{B}}^{-1}{\bm{b}}\left(\frac{1}{T}\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{H}{\bm{z}}\right)^{\top}{\bm{B}}^{-1}}{1+\sigma_{f}\sigma_{\epsilon}\left(\frac{1}{T}\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{H}{\bm{z}}\right)^{\top}{\bm{B}}^{-1}{\bm{b}}}\right]^{-1}{\bm{b}}

\displaystyle={\tilde{\beta}}_{B1}-\frac{{\sigma_{f}}{\sigma_{\epsilon}}{\tilde{\beta}}_{B1}\beta_{B1}}{1+{\sigma_{f}}{\sigma_{\epsilon}}\beta_{B1}}=O_{p}(T^{-\frac{1}{2}}).

Limit of
{\tilde{\tilde{\alpha}}}_{{\widehat{\Sigma}}1}
:

\displaystyle{\tilde{\tilde{\alpha}}}_{{\widehat{\Sigma}}1}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{1}{T}\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{e}\right)^{\top}{\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}}^{-1}\left(\frac{1}{T}\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{e}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{1}{T}\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{e}\right)^{\top}\left[{\bm{A}}^{-1}-\frac{\sigma_{f}^{2}{\bm{A}}^{-1}{\bm{b}}{\bm{b}}^{\top}{\bm{A}}^{-1}}{1+\sigma_{f}^{2}{\bm{b}}^{\top}{\bm{A}}^{-1}{\bm{b}}}\right]^{-1}\left(\frac{1}{T}\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{e}\right)

\displaystyle={\tilde{\tilde{\alpha}}}_{A1}-\frac{{\sigma_{f}^{2}}{\tilde{\beta}}_{A1}{\tilde{\beta}}_{A1}}{1+{\sigma_{f}^{2}}\gamma_{A1}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle i.p.}}{{\longrightarrow}}\frac{1}{\sigma_{\epsilon}^{2}}\frac{\rho}{1-\rho}.

Limit of
{\tilde{\beta}}_{{\widehat{\Sigma}}1}
:

\displaystyle{\tilde{\beta}}_{{\widehat{\Sigma}}1}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{1}{T}\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{e}\right)^{\top}{\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}}^{-1}{\bm{b}}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{1}{T}\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{e}\right)^{\top}\left[{\bm{A}}^{-1}-\frac{\sigma_{f}^{2}{\bm{A}}^{-1}{\bm{b}}{\bm{b}}^{\top}{\bm{A}}^{-1}}{1+\sigma_{f}^{2}{\bm{b}}^{\top}{\bm{A}}^{-1}{\bm{b}}}\right]^{-1}{\bm{b}}

\displaystyle=\frac{{\tilde{\beta}}_{A1}}{1+{\sigma_{f}^{2}}\gamma_{A1}}=O_{p}(T^{-\frac{1}{2}}).

Limit of
\gamma_{S2}
: By the same justification in
D4
, we have

{\bm{b}}^{\top}{\bm{\mathcal{S}}}^{-2}{\bm{b}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle i.p.}}{{\longrightarrow}}\dfrac{\tilde{b}^{2}}{(1-\rho)^{3}}=d.

Limit of
\beta_{S2}
: Consider the square of
\beta_{S2}
, we have

T\beta_{S2}^{2}=\frac{1}{T}{\bm{z}}^{\top}\bm{H}\bm{Y}{\bm{\mathcal{S}}}^{-2}{\bm{b}}{\bm{b}}^{\top}{\bm{\mathcal{S}}}^{-2}\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{H}{\bm{z}}.

Based on trace Lemma
1
, we have

\displaystyle T\beta_{S2}^{2}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{T}{\bm{b}}^{\top}{\bm{\mathcal{S}}}^{-2}\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{H}\bm{Y}{\bm{\mathcal{S}}}^{-2}{\bm{b}}=O_{p}(T^{-\frac{1}{2}}),

\displaystyle T\beta_{S2}^{2}

\displaystyle-{\bm{b}}^{\top}{\bm{\mathcal{S}}}^{-3}{\bm{b}}=O_{p}(T^{-\frac{1}{2}}),

\displaystyle T\beta_{S2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\dfrac{\tilde{b}^{2}\left(1+\rho\right)}{(1-\rho)^{5}}+O_{p}(T^{-\frac{1}{2}}).

Thus,
\beta_{S2}=O_{p}(T^{-\frac{1}{2}})
.

Limit of
\alpha_{S2}
: Based on trace Lemma
1
and the fact that the eigenmatrix of
{\bm{\mathcal{S}}}
is uniformly (i.e., Haar) distributed, we have

\displaystyle\alpha_{S2}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{T}\Tr\left[\frac{1}{T}\bm{H}\bm{Y}{\bm{\mathcal{S}}}^{-2}\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{H}\right]=O_{p}(T^{-\frac{1}{2}})

\displaystyle\alpha_{S2}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{T}\Tr\left[{\bm{\mathcal{S}}}^{-1}\right]=O_{p}(T^{-\frac{1}{2}}).

Thus,
\alpha_{S2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle i.p.}}{{\longrightarrow}}\frac{\rho}{1-\rho}
.

Limit of
\beta_{B2}
:

\displaystyle\beta_{B2}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{1}{T}\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{H}{\bm{z}}\right)^{\top}{\bm{B}}^{-1}{\bm{B}}^{-1}{\bm{b}}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{1}{T}\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{H}{\bm{z}}\right)^{\top}\left[{\bm{C}}^{-1}-\frac{\frac{\sigma_{f}}{\sigma_{\epsilon}^{3}}{\bm{\mathcal{S}}}^{-1}\left(\frac{1}{T}\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{H}{\bm{z}}\right){\bm{b}}^{\top}{\bm{\mathcal{S}}}^{-1}}{1+\frac{\sigma_{f}}{\sigma_{\epsilon}}{\bm{b}}^{\top}{\bm{\mathcal{S}}}^{-1}\frac{1}{T}\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{H}{\bm{z}}}\right]

\displaystyle\quad\left[{\bm{C}}^{-1}-\frac{\frac{\sigma_{f}}{\sigma_{\epsilon}^{3}}{\bm{\mathcal{S}}}^{-1}\left(\frac{1}{T}\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{H}{\bm{z}}\right){\bm{b}}^{\top}{\bm{\mathcal{S}}}^{-1}}{1+\frac{\sigma_{f}}{\sigma_{\epsilon}}{\bm{b}}^{\top}{\bm{\mathcal{S}}}^{-1}\frac{1}{T}\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{H}{\bm{z}}}\right]{\bm{b}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sigma_{\epsilon}^{4}}\beta_{S2}-\frac{\frac{\sigma_{f}}{\sigma_{\epsilon}^{5}}\alpha_{S2}\gamma_{S1}}{1+\frac{\sigma_{f}}{\sigma_{\epsilon}}\beta_{S1}}-\frac{\frac{\sigma_{f}}{\sigma_{\epsilon}^{5}}\alpha_{S1}\gamma_{S2}}{1+\frac{\sigma_{f}}{\sigma_{\epsilon}}\beta_{S1}}+\frac{\frac{\sigma_{f}^{2}}{\sigma_{\epsilon}^{6}}\alpha_{S1}\beta_{S2}\gamma_{S1}}{(1+\frac{\sigma_{f}}{\sigma_{\epsilon}}\beta_{S1})^{2}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle i.p.}}{{\longrightarrow}}-\frac{\sigma_{f}}{\sigma_{\epsilon}^{5}}\frac{\rho}{1-\rho}c-\frac{\sigma_{f}}{\sigma_{\epsilon}^{5}}\rho d=-\frac{\sigma_{f}}{\sigma_{\epsilon}^{5}}\tilde{b}^{2}\frac{2\rho-\rho^{2}}{(1-\rho)^{3}}.

Limit of
\gamma_{B2}
:

\displaystyle\gamma_{B2}

\displaystyle={\bm{b}}^{\top}{\bm{B}}^{-1}{\bm{B}}^{-1}{\bm{b}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sigma_{\epsilon}^{4}}\gamma_{S2}-\frac{\frac{\sigma_{f}}{\sigma_{\epsilon}^{5}}\beta_{S2}\gamma_{S1}}{1+\frac{\sigma_{f}}{\sigma_{\epsilon}}\beta_{S1}}-\frac{\frac{\sigma_{f}}{\sigma_{\epsilon}^{5}}\beta_{S1}\gamma_{S2}}{1+\frac{\sigma_{f}}{\sigma_{\epsilon}}\beta_{S1}}+\frac{\frac{\sigma_{f}^{2}}{\sigma_{\epsilon}^{6}}\beta_{S1}\beta_{S2}\gamma_{S1}}{(1+\frac{\sigma_{f}}{\sigma_{\epsilon}}\beta_{S1})^{2}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle i.p.}}{{\longrightarrow}}\frac{1}{\sigma_{\epsilon}^{4}}d=\frac{1}{\sigma_{\epsilon}^{4}}\tilde{b}^{2}\frac{1}{(1-\rho)^{3}}.

Limit of
\gamma_{A2}
:

\displaystyle\gamma_{A2}

\displaystyle={\bm{b}}^{\top}{\bm{A}}^{-1}{\bm{A}}^{-1}{\bm{b}}

\displaystyle={\bm{b}}^{\top}\left[{\bm{B}}^{-1}-\frac{\sigma_{f}\sigma_{\epsilon}{\bm{B}}^{-1}{\bm{b}}\left(\frac{1}{T}\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{H}{\bm{z}}\right)^{\top}{\bm{B}}^{-1}}{1+\sigma_{f}\sigma_{\epsilon}\beta_{B1}}\right]

\displaystyle\quad\left[{\bm{B}}^{-1}-\frac{\sigma_{f}\sigma_{\epsilon}{\bm{B}}^{-1}{\bm{b}}\left(\frac{1}{T}\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{H}{\bm{z}}\right)^{\top}{\bm{B}}^{-1}}{1+\sigma_{f}\sigma_{\epsilon}\beta_{B1}}\right]{\bm{b}}

\displaystyle=\gamma_{B2}-\frac{{\sigma_{f}}{\sigma_{\epsilon}}\gamma_{B2}\beta_{B1}}{1+{\sigma_{f}}{\sigma_{\epsilon}}\beta_{B1}}-\frac{{\sigma_{f}}{\sigma_{\epsilon}}\gamma_{B1}\beta_{B2}}{1+{\sigma_{f}}{\sigma_{\epsilon}}\beta_{B1}}+\frac{{\sigma_{f}^{2}}{\sigma_{\epsilon}^{2}}\gamma_{B1}\beta_{B2}\beta_{B1}}{(1+{\sigma_{f}}{\sigma_{\epsilon}}\beta_{B1})^{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\gamma_{B2}+\sigma_{f}\sigma_{\epsilon}\gamma_{B2}\beta_{B1}-\sigma_{f}\sigma_{\epsilon}\beta_{B2}\gamma_{B1}}{(1+{\sigma_{f}}{\sigma_{\epsilon}}\beta_{B1})^{2}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle i.p.}}{{\longrightarrow}}\frac{\frac{1}{\sigma_{\epsilon}^{4}}d\left[1+\frac{\sigma_{f}^{2}}{\sigma_{\epsilon}^{2}}\rho(1-\rho)^{3}d\right]}{\left[1-\frac{\sigma_{f}^{2}}{\sigma_{\epsilon}^{2}}\rho c\right]^{2}}.

Limit of
\gamma_{{\widehat{\Sigma}}2}
:

\displaystyle\gamma_{{\widehat{\Sigma}}2}

\displaystyle={\bm{b}}^{\top}{\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}}^{-1}{\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}}^{-1}{\bm{b}}

\displaystyle={\bm{b}}^{\top}\left[{\bm{A}}^{-1}-\frac{\sigma_{f}^{2}{\bm{A}}^{-1}{\bm{b}}{\bm{b}}^{\top}{\bm{A}}^{-1}}{1+\sigma_{f}^{2}\gamma_{A1}}\right]\left[{\bm{A}}^{-1}-\frac{\sigma_{f}^{2}{\bm{A}}^{-1}{\bm{b}}{\bm{b}}^{\top}{\bm{A}}^{-1}}{1+\sigma_{f}^{2}\gamma_{A1}}\right]{\bm{b}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\gamma_{A2}}{(1+\sigma_{f}^{2}\gamma_{A1})^{2}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle i.p.}}{{\longrightarrow}}\frac{\frac{1}{\sigma_{\epsilon}^{4}}d\left[1+\frac{\sigma_{f}^{2}}{\sigma_{\epsilon}^{2}}\rho(1-\rho)^{3}d\right]}{\left[1-\frac{\sigma_{f}^{2}}{\sigma_{\epsilon}^{2}}\rho c+\frac{\sigma_{f}^{2}}{\sigma_{\epsilon}^{2}}c\right]^{2}}.

Limit of
{\tilde{\alpha}}_{S2}
: Consider the square of
{\tilde{\alpha}}_{S2}
, we have

T{\tilde{\alpha}}_{S2}^{2}=\frac{1}{T}{\bm{z}}^{\top}\bm{H}\bm{Y}\frac{1}{T}{\bm{\mathcal{S}}}^{-2}\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{e}\bm{e}^{\top}\bm{Y}{\bm{\mathcal{S}}}^{-2}\frac{1}{T}\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{H}{\bm{z}}.

Based on trace Lemma
1
, we have

\displaystyle T{\tilde{\alpha}}_{S2}^{2}

\displaystyle-\left(\frac{1}{T}\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{e}\right)^{\top}{\bm{\mathcal{S}}}^{-3}\left(\frac{1}{T}\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{e}\right)=O_{p}(T^{-\frac{1}{2}}),

\displaystyle T{\tilde{\alpha}}_{S2}^{2}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle i.p.}}{{\longrightarrow}}\frac{(1+\rho)\rho}{(1-\rho)^{5}}.

Thus,
{\tilde{\alpha}}_{S2}=O_{p}(T^{-\frac{1}{2}})
.

Limit of
{\tilde{\tilde{\alpha}}}_{S2}
: By the same justification in
D5
, we have

{\tilde{\tilde{\alpha}}}_{S2}=\left(\frac{1}{T}\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{e}\right)^{\top}{\bm{\mathcal{S}}}^{-2}\left(\frac{1}{T}\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{e}\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle i.p.}}{{\longrightarrow}}\frac{\rho}{(1-\rho)^{3}}.

Limit of
{\tilde{\beta}}_{S2}
: By the same justification in
D6
, we have

\displaystyle{\tilde{\beta}}_{S2}=\left(\frac{1}{T}\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{e}\right)^{\top}{\bm{\mathcal{S}}}^{-2}{\bm{b}}=O_{p}(T^{-\frac{1}{2}}).

Limit of
{\tilde{\tilde{\alpha}}}_{B2}
:

\displaystyle{\tilde{\tilde{\alpha}}}_{B2}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{1}{T}\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{e}\right)^{\top}{\bm{B}}^{-1}{\bm{B}}^{-1}\left(\frac{1}{T}\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{e}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{1}{T}\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{e}\right)^{\top}\left[{\bm{C}}^{-1}-\frac{\frac{\sigma_{f}}{\sigma_{\epsilon}^{3}}{\bm{\mathcal{S}}}^{-1}\left(\frac{1}{T}\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{H}{\bm{z}}\right){\bm{b}}^{\top}{\bm{\mathcal{S}}}^{-1}}{1+\frac{\sigma_{f}}{\sigma_{\epsilon}}{\bm{b}}^{\top}{\bm{\mathcal{S}}}^{-1}\frac{1}{T}\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{H}{\bm{z}}}\right]

\displaystyle\quad\left[{\bm{C}}^{-1}-\frac{\frac{\sigma_{f}}{\sigma_{\epsilon}^{3}}{\bm{\mathcal{S}}}^{-1}\left(\frac{1}{T}\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{H}{\bm{z}}\right){\bm{b}}^{\top}{\bm{\mathcal{S}}}^{-1}}{1+\frac{\sigma_{f}}{\sigma_{\epsilon}}{\bm{b}}^{\top}{\bm{\mathcal{S}}}^{-1}\frac{1}{T}\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{H}{\bm{z}}}\right]\left(\frac{1}{T}\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{e}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sigma_{\epsilon}^{4}}{\tilde{\tilde{\alpha}}}_{S2}+O_{p}(T^{-\frac{1}{2}})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle i.p.}}{{\longrightarrow}}\frac{1}{\sigma_{\epsilon}^{4}}\frac{\rho}{(1-\rho)^{3}}.

Limit of
{\tilde{\beta}}_{B2}
:

\displaystyle{\tilde{\beta}}_{B2}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{1}{T}\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{e}\right)^{\top}{\bm{B}}^{-1}{\bm{B}}^{-1}{\bm{b}}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{1}{T}\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{e}\right)^{\top}\left[{\bm{C}}^{-1}-\frac{\frac{\sigma_{f}}{\sigma_{\epsilon}^{3}}{\bm{\mathcal{S}}}^{-1}\left(\frac{1}{T}\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{H}{\bm{z}}\right){\bm{b}}^{\top}{\bm{\mathcal{S}}}^{-1}}{1+\frac{\sigma_{f}}{\sigma_{\epsilon}}{\bm{b}}^{\top}{\bm{\mathcal{S}}}^{-1}\frac{1}{T}\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{H}{\bm{z}}}\right]

\displaystyle\quad\left[{\bm{C}}^{-1}-\frac{\frac{\sigma_{f}}{\sigma_{\epsilon}^{3}}{\bm{\mathcal{S}}}^{-1}\left(\frac{1}{T}\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{H}{\bm{z}}\right){\bm{b}}^{\top}{\bm{\mathcal{S}}}^{-1}}{1+\frac{\sigma_{f}}{\sigma_{\epsilon}}{\bm{b}}^{\top}{\bm{\mathcal{S}}}^{-1}\frac{1}{T}\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{H}{\bm{z}}}\right]{\bm{b}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sigma_{\epsilon}^{4}}{\tilde{\beta}}_{S2}-\frac{\frac{\sigma_{f}}{\sigma_{\epsilon}^{5}}{\tilde{\alpha}}_{S2}\gamma_{S1}}{1+\frac{\sigma_{f}}{\sigma_{\epsilon}}\beta_{S1}}-\frac{\frac{\sigma_{f}}{\sigma_{\epsilon}^{5}}{\tilde{\alpha}}_{S1}\gamma_{S2}}{1+\frac{\sigma_{f}}{\sigma_{\epsilon}}\beta_{S1}}+\frac{\frac{\sigma_{f}^{2}}{\sigma_{\epsilon}^{6}}{\tilde{\alpha}}_{S1}\beta_{S2}\gamma_{S1}}{(1+\frac{\sigma_{f}}{\sigma_{\epsilon}}\beta_{S1})^{2}}

\displaystyle=O_{p}(T^{-\frac{1}{2}}).

Limit of
{\tilde{\alpha}}_{B2}
:

\displaystyle{\tilde{\alpha}}_{B2}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{1}{T}\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{H}{\bm{z}}\right)^{\top}{\bm{B}}^{-1}{\bm{B}}^{-1}\left(\frac{1}{T}\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{e}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{1}{T}\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{H}{\bm{z}}\right)^{\top}\left[{\bm{C}}^{-1}-\frac{\frac{\sigma_{f}}{\sigma_{\epsilon}^{3}}{\bm{\mathcal{S}}}^{-1}\left(\frac{1}{T}\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{H}{\bm{z}}\right){\bm{b}}^{\top}{\bm{\mathcal{S}}}^{-1}}{1+\frac{\sigma_{f}}{\sigma_{\epsilon}}{\bm{b}}^{\top}{\bm{\mathcal{S}}}^{-1}\frac{1}{T}\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{H}{\bm{z}}}\right]

\displaystyle\quad\left[{\bm{C}}^{-1}-\frac{\frac{\sigma_{f}}{\sigma_{\epsilon}^{3}}{\bm{\mathcal{S}}}^{-1}\left(\frac{1}{T}\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{H}{\bm{z}}\right){\bm{b}}^{\top}{\bm{\mathcal{S}}}^{-1}}{1+\frac{\sigma_{f}}{\sigma_{\epsilon}}{\bm{b}}^{\top}{\bm{\mathcal{S}}}^{-1}\frac{1}{T}\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{H}{\bm{z}}}\right]\left(\frac{1}{T}\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{e}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sigma_{\epsilon}^{4}}{\tilde{\alpha}}_{S2}-\frac{\frac{\sigma_{f}}{\sigma_{\epsilon}^{5}}\alpha_{S2}{\tilde{\beta}}_{S1}}{1+\frac{\sigma_{f}}{\sigma_{\epsilon}}\beta_{S1}}-\frac{\frac{\sigma_{f}}{\sigma_{\epsilon}^{5}}\alpha_{S1}{\tilde{\beta}}_{S2}}{1+\frac{\sigma_{f}}{\sigma_{\epsilon}}\beta_{S1}}+\frac{\frac{\sigma_{f}^{2}}{\sigma_{\epsilon}^{6}}\alpha_{S1}\beta_{S2}{\tilde{\beta}}_{S1}}{(1+\frac{\sigma_{f}}{\sigma_{\epsilon}}\beta_{S1})^{2}}

\displaystyle=O_{p}(T^{-\frac{1}{2}}).

Limit of
{\tilde{\tilde{\alpha}}}_{A2}
:

\displaystyle{\tilde{\tilde{\alpha}}}_{A2}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{1}{T}\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{e}\right)^{\top}{\bm{A}}^{-1}{\bm{A}}^{-1}\left(\frac{1}{T}\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{e}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{1}{T}\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{e}\right)^{\top}\left[{\bm{B}}^{-1}-\frac{\sigma_{f}\sigma_{\epsilon}{\bm{B}}^{-1}{\bm{b}}\left(\frac{1}{T}\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{H}{\bm{z}}\right)^{\top}{\bm{B}}^{-1}}{1+\sigma_{f}\sigma_{\epsilon}\beta_{B1}}\right]

\displaystyle\quad\left[{\bm{B}}^{-1}-\frac{\sigma_{f}\sigma_{\epsilon}{\bm{B}}^{-1}{\bm{b}}\left(\frac{1}{T}\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{H}{\bm{z}}\right)^{\top}{\bm{B}}^{-1}}{1+\sigma_{f}\sigma_{\epsilon}\beta_{B1}}\right]\left(\frac{1}{T}\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{e}\right)

\displaystyle={\tilde{\tilde{\alpha}}}_{B2}-\frac{{\sigma_{f}}{\sigma_{\epsilon}}{\tilde{\beta}}_{B2}{\tilde{\alpha}}_{B1}}{1+{\sigma_{f}}{\sigma_{\epsilon}}\beta_{B1}}-\frac{{\sigma_{f}}{\sigma_{\epsilon}}{\tilde{\beta}}_{B1}{\tilde{\alpha}}_{B2}}{1+{\sigma_{f}}{\sigma_{\epsilon}}\beta_{B1}}+\frac{{\sigma_{f}^{2}}{\sigma_{\epsilon}^{2}}{\tilde{\beta}}_{B1}\beta_{B2}{\tilde{\alpha}}_{B1}}{(1+{\sigma_{f}}{\sigma_{\epsilon}}\beta_{B1})^{2}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle i.p.}}{{\longrightarrow}}\frac{1}{\sigma_{\epsilon}^{4}}\frac{\rho}{(1-\rho)^{3}}.

Limit of
{\tilde{\beta}}_{A2}
:

\displaystyle{\tilde{\beta}}_{A2}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{1}{T}\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{e}\right)^{\top}{\bm{A}}^{-1}{\bm{A}}^{-1}{\bm{b}}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{1}{T}\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{e}\right)^{\top}\left[{\bm{B}}^{-1}-\frac{\sigma_{f}\sigma_{\epsilon}{\bm{B}}^{-1}{\bm{b}}\left(\frac{1}{T}\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{H}{\bm{z}}\right)^{\top}{\bm{B}}^{-1}}{1+\sigma_{f}\sigma_{\epsilon}\beta_{B1}}\right]

\displaystyle\quad\left[{\bm{B}}^{-1}-\frac{\sigma_{f}\sigma_{\epsilon}{\bm{B}}^{-1}{\bm{b}}\left(\frac{1}{T}\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{H}{\bm{z}}\right)^{\top}{\bm{B}}^{-1}}{1+\sigma_{f}\sigma_{\epsilon}\beta_{B1}}\right]{\bm{b}}

\displaystyle={\tilde{\beta}}_{B2}-\frac{{\sigma_{f}}{\sigma_{\epsilon}}{\tilde{\beta}}_{B2}\beta_{B1}}{1+{\sigma_{f}}{\sigma_{\epsilon}}\beta_{B1}}-\frac{{\sigma_{f}}{\sigma_{\epsilon}}{\tilde{\beta}}_{B1}\beta_{B2}}{1+{\sigma_{f}}{\sigma_{\epsilon}}\beta_{B1}}+\frac{{\sigma_{f}^{2}}{\sigma_{\epsilon}^{2}}{\tilde{\beta}}_{B1}\beta_{B2}\beta_{B1}}{(1+{\sigma_{f}}{\sigma_{\epsilon}}\beta_{B1})^{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{{\tilde{\beta}}_{B2}+\sigma_{f}\sigma_{\epsilon}\beta_{B1}{\tilde{\beta}}_{B2}-\sigma_{f}\sigma_{\epsilon}{\tilde{\beta}}_{B1}\beta_{B2}}{(1+{\sigma_{f}}{\sigma_{\epsilon}}\beta_{B1})^{2}}=O_{p}(T^{-\frac{1}{2}}).

Limit of
{\tilde{\tilde{\alpha}}}_{{\widehat{\Sigma}}2}
:

\displaystyle{\tilde{\tilde{\alpha}}}_{{\widehat{\Sigma}}2}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{1}{T}\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{e}\right)^{\top}{\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}}^{-1}{\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}}^{-1}\left(\frac{1}{T}\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{e}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{1}{T}\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{e}\right)^{\top}\left[{\bm{A}}^{-1}-\frac{\sigma_{f}^{2}{\bm{A}}^{-1}{\bm{b}}{\bm{b}}^{\top}{\bm{A}}^{-1}}{1+\sigma_{f}^{2}\gamma_{A1}}\right]\left[{\bm{A}}^{-1}-\frac{\sigma_{f}^{2}{\bm{A}}^{-1}{\bm{b}}{\bm{b}}^{\top}{\bm{A}}^{-1}}{1+\sigma_{f}^{2}\gamma_{A1}}\right]\left(\frac{1}{T}\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{e}\right)

\displaystyle={\tilde{\tilde{\alpha}}}_{A2}-\frac{\sigma_{f}^{2}{\tilde{\beta}}_{A2}{\tilde{\beta}}_{A1}}{1+\sigma_{f}^{2}\gamma_{A1}}-\frac{\sigma_{f}^{2}{\tilde{\beta}}_{A1}{\tilde{\beta}}_{A2}}{1+\sigma_{f}^{2}\gamma_{A1}}+\frac{{\sigma_{f}^{4}}{\tilde{\beta}}_{A1}\gamma_{A2}{\tilde{\beta}}_{A1}}{(1+\sigma_{f}^{2}\gamma_{A1})^{2}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle i.p.}}{{\longrightarrow}}\frac{1}{\sigma_{\epsilon}^{4}}\frac{\rho}{(1-\rho)^{3}}.

Limit of
{\tilde{\beta}}_{{\widehat{\Sigma}}2}
:

\displaystyle{\tilde{\beta}}_{{\widehat{\Sigma}}2}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{1}{T}\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{e}\right)^{\top}{\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}}^{-1}{\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}}^{-1}{\bm{b}}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{1}{T}\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{e}\right)^{\top}\left[{\bm{A}}^{-1}-\frac{\sigma_{f}^{2}{\bm{A}}^{-1}{\bm{b}}{\bm{b}}^{\top}{\bm{A}}^{-1}}{1+\sigma_{f}^{2}\gamma_{A1}}\right]\left[{\bm{A}}^{-1}-\frac{\sigma_{f}^{2}{\bm{A}}^{-1}{\bm{b}}{\bm{b}}^{\top}{\bm{A}}^{-1}}{1+\sigma_{f}^{2}\gamma_{A1}}\right]{\bm{b}}

\displaystyle={\tilde{\beta}}_{A2}-\frac{\sigma_{f}^{2}\gamma_{A2}{\tilde{\beta}}_{A1}}{1+\sigma_{f}^{2}\gamma_{A1}}-\frac{\sigma_{f}^{2}\gamma_{A1}{\tilde{\beta}}_{A2}}{1+\sigma_{f}^{2}\gamma_{A1}}+\frac{{\sigma_{f}^{4}}\gamma_{A1}\gamma_{A2}{\tilde{\beta}}_{A1}}{(1+\sigma_{f}^{2}\gamma_{A1})^{2}}

\displaystyle=O_{p}(T^{-\frac{1}{2}}).

C.3.2
Case
\rho>1

We first introduce formulas that may be used to obtain the pseudoinverse of a matrix
{\bm{M}}
modified by a rank-1 matrix. These formulas were developed in
Meyer, (1973)
. For a matrix
{\bm{M}}
, vectors
{\bm{m}}
,
{\bm{n}}
, we adopt the following notation:

\left\|\cdot\right\|
- the Euclidean norm,

R(\cdot)
- the range or column space,

\bm{k}
- the column
{\bm{M}}^{+}{\bm{m}}
,

\bm{h}
- the row
{\bm{n}}^{\top}{\bm{M}}^{+}
,

{\bm{u}}
- the column
\left(\bm{I}_{N}-{\bm{M}}{\bm{M}}^{+}\right){\bm{m}}
,

{\bm{v}}
- the row
{\bm{n}}^{\top}\left(\bm{I}_{N}-{\bm{M}}^{+}{\bm{M}}\right)
,

\beta
- the scalar
1+{\bm{n}}^{\top}{\bm{M}}^{+}{\bm{m}}
.

For a nonzero vector
{\bm{x}}
, its pseudoinverse is given by

{\bm{x}}^{+}=\frac{{\bm{x}}^{\top}}{\left\|{\bm{x}}\right\|^{2}}.

In order to consider the structure of the matrix
\left({\bm{M}}+{\bm{m}}{\bm{n}}^{\top}\right)^{+}
, we list three cases that will be used in the following proofs:

1.

{\bm{m}}\not\in R({\bm{M}})
and
{\bm{n}}\not\in R({\bm{M}}^{\top})
:

\left({\bm{M}}+{\bm{m}}{\bm{n}}^{\top}\right)^{+}={\bm{M}}^{+}-\bm{k}{\bm{u}}^{+}-{\bm{v}}^{+}\bm{h}+\beta{\bm{v}}^{+}{\bm{u}}^{+}.

2.

{\bm{m}}\in R({\bm{M}})
and
{\bm{n}}
arbitrary and
\beta\neq 0
:

\displaystyle{\bm{p}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\left\|\bm{k}\right\|^{2}}{\beta}{\bm{v}}^{\top}-\bm{k},

\displaystyle{\bm{q}}^{\top}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\left\|{\bm{v}}\right\|^{2}}{\beta}\bm{k}^{\top}{\bm{M}}^{+}-\bm{h},

\displaystyle\sigma

\displaystyle=\left\|\bm{k}\right\|^{2}\left\|{\bm{v}}\right\|^{2}+\left|\beta\right|^{2},

\displaystyle\left({\bm{M}}+{\bm{m}}{\bm{n}}^{\top}\right)^{+}

\displaystyle={\bm{M}}^{+}+\frac{1}{\beta}{\bm{v}}^{\top}\bm{k}^{\top}{\bm{M}}^{+}-\frac{\beta}{\sigma}{\bm{p}}{\bm{q}}^{\top}.

3.

{\bm{m}}\in R({\bm{M}})
and
{\bm{n}}\in R({\bm{M}}^{\top})
and
\beta=0
:

\left({\bm{M}}+{\bm{m}}{\bm{n}}^{\top}\right)^{+}={\bm{M}}^{+}-\bm{k}\bm{k}^{+}{\bm{M}}^{+}-{\bm{M}}^{+}\bm{h}^{+}\bm{h}+(\bm{k}^{+}{\bm{M}}^{+}\bm{h}^{+})\bm{k}\bm{h}.

We still consider

\displaystyle{\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}}=\sigma_{\epsilon}^{2}{\bm{\mathcal{S}}}+\left(\sigma_{f}\sigma_{\epsilon}\dfrac{1}{T}\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{H}{\bm{z}}\right){\bm{b}}^{\top}+{\bm{b}}\left(\sigma_{f}\sigma_{\epsilon}\dfrac{1}{T}\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{H}{\bm{z}}\right)^{\top}+\sigma_{f}^{2}{\bm{b}}{\bm{b}}^{\top}.

Denote

\displaystyle{\bm{C}}

\displaystyle=\sigma_{\epsilon}^{2}{\bm{\mathcal{S}}},

\displaystyle{\bm{B}}

\displaystyle={\bm{C}}+\left(\sigma_{f}\sigma_{\epsilon}\frac{1}{T}\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{H}{\bm{z}}\right){\bm{b}}^{\top},

\displaystyle{\bm{A}}

\displaystyle={\bm{B}}+{\bm{b}}\left(\sigma_{f}\sigma_{\epsilon}\dfrac{1}{T}\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{H}{\bm{z}}\right)^{\top},

it is easy to verify that we need formula in case 2 to calculate
{\bm{B}}^{+}
, formula in case 1 to calculate
{\bm{A}}^{+}
and formula in case 3 to calculate
{\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}}^{+}
. In the following, we present pseudoinverse of matrices
{\bm{B}},{\bm{A}}
and
{\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}}
:

{\bm{B}}^{+}
:

\displaystyle\beta_{{\bm{B}}}

\displaystyle={\bm{B}}^{+}{\bm{b}}

:

\displaystyle\beta_{{\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}}}

\displaystyle=1+\sigma_{f}^{2}{\bm{b}}^{\top}{\bm{A}}^{+}{\bm{b}}=1+\sigma_{f}^{2}{\underline{\gamma}}_{A1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle i.p.}}{{\longrightarrow}}0

\displaystyle=\sigma_{f}^{2}{\bm{A}}^{+}{\bm{b}}

\displaystyle={\bm{b}}^{\top}{\bm{A}}^{+}
